# obop decision register

**Status:** Draft 0.1. The owner rulings OR-1 to OR-21 of 2026-10-01 (OR-1 to OR-13 first, OR-14 to OR-21 later the same day) and OR-22 to OR-34 of 2026-10-27 are recorded below as RATIFIED and override every Proposed position in this register, the companion documents and the research; everything the owner has not ruled still awaits ratification. **As of:** 2026-10-01, called month 0 (M0); the rulings of 2026-10-27 and the records D40 to D79 (the build) are recorded as of their own dates. **Scope:** product and architecture. Nothing here is legal advice; items marked "counsel" need a lawyer.

## 0. Front matter and how to read

This register is authoritative for every other obop document. Where a research note (R01-R28, index R00) disagrees with it, this file wins. Where it is silent, the research stands and later notes outrank earlier ones (R23, R24 and R26 outrank the rest), except where an owner ruling (next section) has overridden them: the research of R17 (licensing), R24 s9-s11 (marketplace commerce), R26 (pricing, funding, risk model) and R20 (go-to-market) is now largely historical, and section 7 lists what not to repeat. Module-level design rules (the MUST lists of R09-R16 on ledger, stock, pricing, subscriptions, POS, payroll, services) are not repeated here; module design documents must restate them and cite the R-tag. [CONVENTIONS.md](CONVENTIONS.md) fixes the spelling of names that cross documents (events, permission groups, data classes, states, ports); where it differs from this register, this register wins, and it never settles an owner question. Early code exists (the code map is in CLAUDE.md); the records of the build (D40 to D79) describe what is built, the other records are mostly design.

| Status | Meaning | Rule for writers |
|---|---|---|
| FIXED | Owner-mandated constraint | Never changes without the owner |
| RATIFIED | Owner ruling of 2026-10-01 or 2026-10-27 (OR-1 to OR-34), recorded as decided; it is binding in effect and keeps this label so the origin stays visible | State as fact and cite the OR number; a later owner ruling may change it, nothing else |
| BINDING | Research-resolved technical decision | State as fact; changing it needs a new ADR (next free D-ID; the old record is marked "Superseded by") |
| PROPOSED | Needs explicit owner ratification | Write "Proposed (Dn)" wherever you rely on it |
| DEFERRED | Decide later, with a trigger | Do not design around a guess; cite the trigger |
| SUPERSEDED | Replaced by a later record or ruling; kept as a stub for the trail | Never rely on it; follow the pointer |

Conventions. "R23 DR-1" means decision record 1 in research note 23 (section 9 maps tags to files). Dates are ISO-8601, money is EUR ex VAT, "M9" is month 9 after M0. Reversibility is "one-way door" (cannot be undone cheaply once data, licences or outsiders depend on it) or "two-way door". The owner ratifies a PROPOSED record by saying so in chat; the record then gains a "Ratified" line and becomes BINDING (records ratified on 2026-10-01 carry the label RATIFIED and an OR number). Each rewritten record keeps one "Superseded alternative" line naming what it replaced. Documents must never write "GA" bare; see D26. Wave (W0-W4), release-number and calendar references inside a BINDING record are placements under D25 (PROPOSED) and D26 (the wave windows, the German pack date and the 1.0 target are RATIFIED by OR-13; every other date stays PROPOSED): the technical rule is BINDING, the placement is Proposed unless D26 says otherwise. Where a record's own clause rests on a Proposed item (D25 on the T0 list, D81 on its staging, D85 on its cadence, D80, D83 and D86 on the details they mark Proposed), the record says so.

## Owner rulings (2026-10-01)

<a id="owner-rulings"></a>

**Status: RATIFIED.** The owner gave the twenty-one rulings below in chat on 2026-10-01: OR-1 to OR-13 first (batch 1) and OR-14 to OR-21 later the same day (batch 2). Quotation marks are the owner's own words; the other rulings, all of batch 2 included, are recorded as relayed. They override every Proposed position in this register, in the companion documents and in the research. The rulings were first drafted in a working copy that did not land with the licence rulings; they are recorded here on 2026-10-27 (OR-25), under the decision numbers D80 to D86 and the question numbers Q22 to Q36 (see "Landing and numbering" below). Where a ruling says SpaceCorps hosts nothing for others today or has no revenue model, OR-22 of 2026-10-27 overrides it. The documents were written before the rulings and many assume the opposite (AGPL, a closed paid list, plans and prices, a funded team, a 5-year LTS, a local-only demo instead of a hosted sandbox); where any document disagrees with this section, this section wins, and section 7 lists what not to repeat. Each ruling is identified as OR-1 to OR-21 and is cited by that number.

| OR | Ruling | Decisions and questions touched | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| OR-1 | **Licence.** The whole project is Apache-2.0 (permissive, no copyleft layer). Contributor terms: DCO sign-off, no CLA. LICENSE and NOTICE exist at the repository root ("Copyright 2026 SpaceCorps contributors"). | F4, F5, D1, D11, D15, D32, D82; Q1, Q16 | No AGPL core, no s7 Application Exception, no Apache-2.0-only-for-SDKs split, no `ee` tree, no licence-grant CLA, no OSI-forever pledge, no steward veto, no investor covenant. The extension boundary is no longer a copyleft question. The dependency allow-list excludes copyleft-only components. Every crate, bundled package and JavaScript manifest of this repository carries the SPDX identifier `Apache-2.0`. DCO sign-off on agent-authored commits: OR-18. |
| OR-2 | **No proprietary paid software at all.** Owner: "completely free to use, you can self host". No open-core, no enterprise layer, no closed or ratcheting paid list, no paid plans, no price list, no per-seat or per-organisation pricing, no paid partner programme or marketplace commission. There is no revenue model today; any future services (support, hosting, certification) would be separate and must never restrict the free software. **Since OR-22 (2026-10-27)** one such service is planned: a paid hosting service run by SpaceCorps. | F5, D2, D17, D18, D19, D26, D31, D32, D38, section 6; Q3, Q13, Q19 | The paid list, ratchet, conversion rule and operated-service lines are deleted. So are Free, Team, Growth and Scale, every price and price test, the Assurance and payroll services, the SLA plan, hosted AI credits, certification fees, partner and marketplace revenue shares and the white-label licence. Every former paid-list capability is an ordinary backlog item: not withheld, not promised. The hosting service of OR-22 is the one exception to "no operated service". |
| OR-3 | **Plugin marketplace.** A free, open registry of plugins. The obop CLI can search, install (version-pinned) or copy the plugin source into the user's own instance ("configure/copy with CLI"). Verified and community trust tiers. obop handles no payments. The staged delivery (a git-backed static index first, a registry service later) is a proposed detail, not yet ruled. | D32, D81; Q13, Q19, Q23 to Q25 | Marketplace commerce (paid listings, payouts, take rate) is gone. D32's five trust levels become private, community, verified and core. Install and copy are the two CLI modes. Admission (an OSI-approved licence) and the meaning of verified: OR-17. |
| OR-4 | **Agentic first, CLI for everything.** Owner: "AGENTIC FIRST EVERYTHING CLI FOR EVERYTHING". Every capability is a typed command reachable through the obop CLI, the REST API and MCP first. The web UI (React on Vite+, driven via `vp` with pnpm) is a client on the same commands and reaches parity with them before the first stable release. A feature is not done until it exists in CLI, API and MCP. | F6, D18, D29, D31, D35, D80; Q22, Q30 | Build order is command first, generated CLI, API and MCP next, UI last. "UI equals API" ordering in older documents is superseded. The spec [spec/cli.md](spec/cli.md) defines the grammar (Proposed until Q22 is answered). Literal UI parity and the exemption list: OR-16. |
| OR-5 | **Name.** "obop" stays the working name; clearance first (counsel opinion by 2026-12-15, else a screened fallback). The GitHub repository SpaceCorps/obop is private and stays private until clearance and the licence and publisher matters are settled (overtaken, OR-26: the owner opened the repository on 2026-10-26; see Repository below). | D3, D26 (G0); Q2, Q16, Q26 | D3 keeps its substance and is RATIFIED. Gate G0 is re-defined around clearance and the publisher matters (D26). |
| OR-6 | **Funding.** Owner: "for now self funded dont worry about this we are GIGA DEVELOPERS YOU AND ME". The team is the owner plus AI agents. No investors, no hiring plan, no ARR gates, no covenants, no grant dependency (grants, for example NLnet, remain an optional idea only). | D5 (superseded), D84; Q4, Q31 | The seed and Series A plan, the 17-FTE cap, the 6-FTE cut, the EUR 1M ARR gate, the M24 viability check, team sizes of 4 to 37 and the investor covenant are deleted. Schedule feasibility for a very small team is a named risk (D84). Grants: OR-21 (no NLnet Restack application now). |
| OR-7 | **First market.** Germany first for the first compliance pack. | D4 (country part), D26; Q8 (country part) | Germany is settled. The ICP, the T0 app list, France second and Poland third stay Proposed. |
| OR-8 | **Pin policy.** "Latest" for Rust and Vite+ means the newest release that has passed a cooldown of 7 to 14 days, or its x.y.1 patch. The toolchain intentionally runs up to about three weeks behind the newest release. | I-3, D23, section 1.1; Q10 | I-3 and D23 are RATIFIED. | OR-9 | **Exceptions to "vp only" (exactly two).** (1) The nightly exit drill, which runs upstream Vite tools without `vp` in a throwaway copy that never ships. "Steward" is reserved for a foundation that may replace SpaceCorps later. |

OR-11 still bars hosting for other people's real data on the free sandbox (the paid hosting service of OR-22 is separate and not yet offered); an optional local demo (`obop dev up`) may still exist. The duties it creates are named in D83: terms of use that forbid personal and production data, a privacy notice, abuse and rate controls, egress and outbound-mail blocking, an auto-reset time to live, sanctions screening, a named small running cost, on-call burden and counsel review. It creates no Data Act switching duty and no SLA, because it holds no customer data and no customer contracts. Q27 is closed; the open details are Q34 to Q36. |
| OR-21 | **No NLnet Restack application now (Q31).** The project is self-funded; revisit when a self-contained reusable crate exists. Grants remain an optional idea only. | D84; Q31 | Q31 is closed, and the NLnet Restack deadline of 2026-11-03 passes without an application. A grant becomes a decision again only when such a crate exists (section 2.3). The funder-terms counsel item (BUSINESS CN-09) stays dormant until then. |

**Repository.** The GitHub repository `SpaceCorps/obop` is public: the owner opened it on 2026-10-26. That replaces the Proposed rule of D26 that it goes public only after gate G0, and the privacy half of OR-5. LICENSE (Apache-2.0) and NOTICE ("Copyright 2026 SpaceCorps contributors") are at the root. OR-26 of 2026-10-27 confirms that this overtook gate G0. Counsel work on the name (D3, Q2), the CRA role (Q5) and the DCO wording (OR-18) stays open and is not a condition of the licence. The initial commit was `f56202e` (research corpus and product description).

**What the rulings do not settle.** Everything in section 8 that the owner did not answer keeps its recommended default and stays PROPOSED: Q6 (spike readout), Q9 (expression storage), Q11 (offline and POS scope), Q12 (Typst only), Q14 (no Node storefront), Q18 (identifier families) and the ICP, T0 list and slip order parts of Q8. The rulings also create new questions, Q22 to Q33 (batch 2 answered Q23, Q27, Q29, Q30 and Q31 and moved Q32 to W2), and OR-20 adds Q34 to Q36. They leave counsel work on the name, the CRA role of SpaceCorps, the trademark policy, the sandbox terms and dependency licences (Q2, Q5, Q16, Q20, Q26, Q34).

**Landing and numbering (OR-25).** The rulings of this section were drafted on 2026-10-01 in a working copy of the initial commit; in the meantime other decisions took the numbers D40 to D79 in this repository, and the code cites them. Main's decisions keep their numbers. The seven decisions the rulings created have new ids, in the same order, and the open questions follow the last question already in the register:

| Drafted as | Recorded as | Title |
|---|---|---|
| D40 | D80 | Agentic-first surface |
| D41 | D81 | Free, open plugin registry |
| D42 | D82 | Contribution model |
| D43 | D83 | Hosting posture and a free sandbox |
| D44 | D84 | Team and funding posture |
| D45 | D85 | Support promise and release train |
| D46 | D86 | W1 scope cut |
| Q21 to Q35 | Q22 to Q36 | Open questions created by the rulings (Q21 is the question of the build, about D40 to D43) |

The OR numbers, F5, F6, I-1 to I-4, CN-numbers and the ids of the other documents are unchanged. Where a ruling or a record below describes the situation of 2026-10-01 (nothing built, the repository private), the record of 2026-10-27 and the code are newer. The same holds for the companion documents: a sentence there that says SpaceCorps hosts nothing for others today, runs no hosted fleet today, sells no hosting yet or has no revenue describes the present (nothing is offered or charged today), and where it reads as a permanent rule, OR-22 wins.

## Owner rulings (2026-10-27): hosting, the exit status, the image

<a id="owner-rulings-2"></a>

**Status: RATIFIED.** The owner gave these rulings in chat on 2026-10-27, answering questions the lead asked directly. Quotation marks are the owner's own words. They continue the numbering of the 2026-10-01 rulings (OR-1 to OR-21 of that day, recorded above; OR-22 was the next free number). OR-22 to OR-33 were given in answer to the lead; OR-34 was given to the main session and relayed to the lead session the same day, as its row says.

| OR | Ruling | Decisions and questions touched | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| OR-22 | **A paid hosting service, nothing else paid.** The software stays completely free (OR-1, OR-2). Owner: "we tell it that it is free, but we can also offer hosting on our side, then we basically offer sliplane dedicated servers, and we just do like 2x or 3x on their price (of sliplane) - find their servers, and charge for additional storage like sliplane does", and "numbers need to be nicely rounded up". Asked which multiple, the owner answered "Use 3x Sliplane's price, rounded up to a price ending in 9." SpaceCorps plans to run, for a business that asks, a dedicated server on Sliplane with obop and PostgreSQL on it. The price is the provider's list price of the server of the same size times three, rounded up to the next price that ends in 9; extra NVMe storage is the provider's block price times three. List prices read on 2026-10-27 (EUR, excluding VAT, per month): 1 vCPU and 1 GB 9.00, 2 and 2 17.80, 3 and 4 28.80, 4 and 8 45.00, 8 and 16 88.20, 16 and 32 224.00, each with 20 GB; extra storage 5.00 per 50 GB. Planned: 29, 59, 89, 139, 269, 679; extra storage 15 per 50 GB. The prices are one table (`web/apps/site/data/hosting.ts`), which also refuses to build if a price falls below three times the provider's. | D2, D83, D84; OR-2, OR-11 | OR-2 stays true for the software: no paid edition, no held-back feature, no per-user fee, no open-core. What changes is that SpaceCorps is no longer assumed to host nothing: the site's Pricing page shows the planned servers, marked "planned, not yet offered or charged" because no billing, terms or processing agreement exists. Nothing is charged and nothing is sold until those exist. Opened for counsel and for the owner before the first sale: SpaceCorps becomes a hosting provider and so a data processor for its customers (a processing agreement, sub-processor disclosure of Sliplane, terms of use and a privacy notice for hosted instances, the Data Act switching duties, the CRA role), VAT and invoicing for a hosting service, a payment provider, the abuse and sanctions approach, the on-call burden of running other people's servers, and Sliplane's terms (they let an account holder give third parties use of its services and remain responsible for them, section 7.1, but say nothing about resale at a mark-up; a written agreement with Sliplane is advisable before the first sale). |
| OR-23 | **Exit status 75 is stable.** Owner: approved. `obop call` and `obop import` exit 75 (`EX_TEMPFAIL`, "try again later") when the database refuses, and print whether anything was changed; every other failed call exits 1. | D74 | The status is part of the stable command-line contract (documented in the CLI reference); scripts may retry on 75 only. |
| OR-24 | **The container image is public.** Owner: make the `ghcr.io/spacecorps/obop` package public. | D59 | The owner changes the package visibility in GitHub (organisation packages settings, "Change visibility"); no agent can. Done on 2026-10-27 (the organisation setting that allows public packages was enabled first); an anonymous pull of `ghcr.io/spacecorps/obop:main` and of the tagged commits answers 200. |
| OR-25 | **The other rulings of 2026-10-01 land, renumbered.** Owner: renumber and land them. | the rulings OR-3 to OR-9, OR-11 to OR-17 and OR-19 to OR-21, and the decisions drafted for them as D40 to D46 | They are recorded above with new ids: D80 to D86 for the decisions and Q22 to Q36 for the questions, as one docs package for the owner's review (table "Landing and numbering"). Where one of them says SpaceCorps hosts nothing for others today (OR-11, D83), OR-22 above wins. |
| OR-26 | **Gate G0 is overtaken.** Owner: "Yes, G0 is overtaken" (asked whether opening the repository on 2026-10-26 should be read as overtaking gate G0 and the "stays private" half of OR-5). | D3, D26 (G0), OR-5; Q2, Q16 | The repository is public by the owner's decision. G0 stays only as a checklist before the 0.3 public alpha: the name cleared or a screened fallback adopted, the publisher matters settled, the DCO check (built when outside contributors appear, OR-30) and the D33 P1 files. Name clearance (D3, Q2) and the counsel items (Q5, Q16, Q26) stay open and no longer gate visibility. |
| OR-27 | **CLI exit codes.** Owner: accepted the proposal. The statuses built today stay stable: 0, 1, 2, 3 (`restore_key_required`), 4 (`backup_not_sealed`), 5 (`restore_files_failed`) and 75 (a refused store, OR-23). The proposed classes of [spec/cli.md](spec/cli.md) 2.4 are numbered 12 auth, 13 denied, 14 invalid and 75 busy (6 conflict, 7 refused, 9 unreachable, 10 approval, 11 partial, 130 interrupted unchanged). | D74, D80; Q22 | The numbers are settled; the commands that would use the new classes are not built, so today every other failed call still exits 1. |
| OR-28 | **pnpm major (Q15): 12.x.** Owner: ratify 12.x. The repository pins `pnpm@12.6.0` (`package.json`). | D23, I-2; Q15 (closed) | The register stops recommending 11.x. The six workspace settings of section 1.1 were behaviourally verified on 11.x only (a parse check passed on 12.8.1), so verifying them on 12.x is open engineering work, not an owner question. |
| OR-29 | **Runner plan (Q17): GitHub-hosted runners.** Owner: withdraw the Hetzner CCX43 proposal; CI stays on GitHub-hosted runners (`ubuntu-latest`, free for a public repository). | D36; Q17 (closed), ENGINEERING Q-E1 | Moving CI off GitHub-hosted runners needs a new owner decision. The cost table of ENGINEERING 3.2 stays as research. |

Which spelling is canonical, and the rest of the grammar of [spec/cli.md](spec/cli.md), stay Proposed under Q22. |
| OR-32 | **Release labels.** Owner: the calendar's 0.1, 0.2 and 0.5 are product milestones (their labels are Proposed); the code's `0.2.0` core version and `v0.2.0-rN` tags are the running build number until the first milestone is announced. | D26, D55; Q8 | Nothing in the code is renamed. The dates of the milestones stay Proposed under Q8. |
| OR-33 | **README.** Owner: keep the short README of this landing. | README.md | The sections of the old README (the name, the reader table, the source list) stay out; the document map and DECISIONS carry them. |
| OR-34 | **React Native (Expo) apps, the mobile-toolchain exception, manual trip recording.** Owner: "React Native (Expo) allowed as an obop client with the mobile-toolchain exception; manual start and stop as the baseline, auto-stop only after legal sign-off; Transistorsoft excluded as the default; Expo SDK 57 for now." The owner gave these words in chat to the main session, and the main session relayed them to the lead session on 2026-10-27. The main session also relayed, not as a quotation, that the owner asked for the decisions to be recorded and the first slice to start. In parts: (a) React Native (Expo) apps are allowed as obop clients, that is as clients of the same API as the web back office. D87 proposes to limit this to first-party apps and to cases a PWA cannot serve; that limit came with the proposal, is not in the owner's words and is Proposed. (b) The mobile-toolchain exception to "`vp` only": the owner said "with the mobile-toolchain exception". It is a third named exception next to the two of OR-9 (the nightly exit drill; `pnpm sbom`). The conditions are the exception as proposed and recorded here: Metro, the Expo CLI, Gradle and Xcode, which `vp` cannot run, are started through `vp env exec` or `vp run` tasks; pnpm stays the only package manager; `vp check`, `vp test` and `vp build` still cover every shared TypeScript package. (c) Manual start and stop is the baseline for recording a trip; auto-stop (an automatic trip end) only after legal sign-off. The proposal adds product and works-council sign-off, and D87 keeps all three as the condition. (d) Transistorsoft is excluded as the default (the owner's words). The proposal's additions are Proposed: the library is react-native-background-geolocation, its release builds need a paid licence key (read on the vendor's README by the lead session on 2026-10-27; the price is not on that page), the reason for the exclusion is that the software must be completely free to build and self-host (OR-1, OR-2), and an optional adapter later is not forbidden. (e) Expo SDK 57 (React Native 0.86) for now. **Amended 2026-10-28** (the owner's answer to the lead's question about the move, relayed by the setup session; not a quotation): start the app on SDK 58 (React Native 0.88) if its packages pass the 7 day cooldown of D23 when the app's build starts (step 5 of the Mileage slice); otherwise stay on SDK 57 and move at the first clean point. This amends "for now"; the move is no longer left open. Step 5 started on 2026-10-28 and the app now pins SDK 58 (React Native 0.88.0, `expo` 58.0.16; docs/spec/mobile.md 8.7); no SDK 57 fallback was needed. (f) Accessibility on a real device (2026-10-28, same relay): a human TalkBack pass is not scheduled now and is not a required step of the real-phone test; the accessibility checklist stays in docs/spec/mobile.md, and until the pass is done the app's accessibility is unchecked on a real device. (g) Where the app's components are shown (Q42, 2026-10-28, relayed by the setup session as Pavel's answer; not a quotation): the normal web Storybook, with the app's components drawn through react-native-web (option 1). The stated downside is part of the ruling: touch, safe areas, fonts and TalkBack are not shown there and are checked on the phone in the acceptance walk. A hidden gallery screen in the app (option 3) only if the web Storybook and the phone disagree; Storybook running inside the app on a device (option 2) only if native-only components dominate, past about 15. Neither option was tested in this repository when it was ruled: the first step of the app's build checks that react-native-web renders the five components (Screen, Button, Row, Field, Banner), and if it fails the question goes back to the owner. That check was run on 2026-10-28: `web/apps/storybook` builds and renders the components through react-native-web in the light and the dark scheme (docs/spec/mobile.md 8.7), so the question did not go back. | D87, D13, D64, D23, D15, D37; OR-1, OR-2, OR-9; Q37 to Q42 | D87 records the decision. Ratified here: the owner's words in parts (a) to (e); the details the proposal added to them are Proposed. Proposed until built: the scope limits (first-party apps only, only where a PWA cannot do the job, the PWA stays the default, Tauri mobile is not pursued), the product and works-council sign-offs for auto-stop, visible recording, the `expo-location` default path and its fallback, the reason given for excluding Transistorsoft and the remark that an optional adapter is not forbidden, the technical design of D87 (the mobile client class of D64, the `device` model, the device outbox of D13, the `mileage` package, the privacy points) and the answers to Q37 to Q41 and Q43 (Q42 is answered by part g). The non-goal "native store apps beyond the PWA and Tauri shells" is replaced by the owner's "allowed as an obop client"; the limits the proposal adds to it are Proposed (PRODUCT 11.3, ux 8, people 8; ARCHITECTURE 10.5 gains the native shell). OR-9's "exactly two" exceptions become three named exceptions, OR-9's two and the mobile toolchain; CLAUDE.md and every place that said "exactly two" are conformed, and no other exception exists. At the time of the ruling nothing was built (since then the first slice has been built up to the Android app, which was run on an emulator and not on a real phone: docs/spec/mobile.md 8.3 to 8.7): the main session relayed on 2026-10-27 that the owner asked for the first slice to start (the Mileage package and app, Android first, development builds, synthetic data only); that request is relayed, not quoted. The ruling gives no agent an Apple or Google account, a store listing or a publication; whether those stay the owner's alone is Proposed (Q37). Auto-stop stays out of every build until the sign-off exists (Q41). |


## 1. Fixed constraints

| ID | Constraint |
|---|---|
| F1 | Backend: exclusively the latest stable Rust, edition 2024. |
| F2 | Frontend and tooling: exclusively the latest Vite+ (`vite-plus`). Every JS/TS task runs through the `vp` CLI. pnpm is the only package manager; `npm`, `npx`, `yarn` and `bun` are never used (CLIs, CI, docs, examples) and `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `bun.lock` are never committed. pnpm still installs from the public npm registry, so naming the registry or a package is fine. Research passages saying Vite+ supports several package managers describe the tool, not policy. |
| F3 | Clean-room relative to Odoo. Odoo is the functional reference. No Enterprise code or assets, no copied Community code, views, translations or assets, no OCA code. Behaviour comes from public docs and Community source read for understanding; charts and rules come from official sources; Odoo may run only as a black-box differential oracle. |
| F4 | Open source: the whole project is OSI-approved open source, under Apache-2.0 (F5, D1). |
| F5 | Licence and price (OR-1, OR-2, OR-22): the whole project (code, SDKs, WIT worlds, UI SDK, templates, documentation) is Apache-2.0 and completely free to use and to self-host. There is no proprietary layer: no open-core, no enterprise tree, no closed or ratcheting paid list, no paid plans or price list, no per-seat or per-organisation pricing, and no paid partner programme or marketplace commission. Contributions carry a DCO sign-off; there is no CLA. The one planned paid thing is a hosting service run by SpaceCorps (OR-22); it and any other future service (support, certification) are separate and never restrict the software. |
| F6 | Agentic first, CLI for everything (OR-4): every capability is a typed command reachable through the `obop` CLI, the REST API and MCP first. The web UI is a client of the same commands and reaches parity with them before the first stable release. A feature is not done until it exists in CLI, API and MCP. |

The identifier family F1-F6 is the owner's fixed constraints; UI tiers F0-F3 are a different family (section 4).

Interpretations, all RATIFIED (I-1 by OR-14, I-2 and I-4 by OR-9, I-3 by OR-8):

- **I-1 (RATIFIED, OR-14).** "Backend exclusively Rust" governs code obop authors and ships. Programs obop only runs or tests against (PostgreSQL, PgBouncer, pgBackRest, OCR engines, LiveKit, Weblate, and validators such as KoSIT, Mustang, phase4 as CI oracles) are dependencies. obop authors no Java, Python or Node server code; off-the-shelf sidecars stay optional and out of the default install. The owner ratified this reading on 2026-10-01 at the recommended default (OR-14); it does not rest on the closure of Q10.
- **I-2 (RATIFIED, OR-9).** "`vp` only" means every JS/TS build, test, lint, format, task and environment command is `vp`; `vp install`, `add` and `remove` reach pnpm, as do the typed `vp pm` subcommands (`audit`, `approve-builds`, `publish`, `stage` and others). `vp pm` is a closed command set, not raw forwarding (checked against vite-plus v1.0.0, source and the installed `vp`, 2026-10-01). Direct `pnpm` is allowed only where `vp` has no wrapper: `vp pm sbom` and `vp pm licenses` do not exist, and `vp pm audit signatures` is rejected and must be written `vp pm audit -- signatures`. The JavaScript SBOM therefore runs as `pnpm sbom` through the vp-managed pnpm, invoked as `vp env exec pnpm sbom ...` or inside a `vp run` task. The owner ruled (OR-9) that this is the second of the two exceptions to "`vp` only" that OR-9 names (OR-34 later added a third, the mobile toolchain), valid until `vp` supports it (the exception lapses when `vp` gains an SBOM wrapper; check at each `vite-plus` upgrade). OR-9 names `pnpm sbom` only, so `pnpm licenses list` is not an exception: the JavaScript licence check reads the licence fields of the SBOM or goes through `vp` (answered by OR-9, Q33). Ad-hoc fetch-and-run is `vp dlx`, never in CI. The CI lint fails on any other JavaScript package-manager lockfile (the Rust `Cargo.lock` is committed and is not in scope), naming `package-lock.json`, `npm-shrinkwrap.json`, `yarn.lock`, `bun.lock` and `bun.lockb` (F2 lists the first, third and fourth; this is the same rule in full).
- **I-3 (RATIFIED, OR-8).** "Latest" means the newest release that has passed a cooldown of 7 to 14 days, or its x.y.1 patch, as section 1.1 spells out. The project therefore intentionally runs up to about three weeks behind the newest release, which is one release behind the literal F1 and F2 wording (Rust 1.98.1 while 1.99.0 was published on 2026-10-01).
- **I-4 (RATIFIED, OR-9).** The nightly exit drill (section 1.1) is the first of the two named exceptions of OR-9 to "`vp` only" (OR-34 added a third, the mobile toolchain, D87): a rehearsal on a throwaway copy of the tree that never ships and is never an obop build path. It is the one place upstream Vite, Vitest, Oxlint and Oxfmt run without `vp`, through pnpm scripts. Shipped builds, CI gates and developer commands always use `vp`. Whether the drill's install step stays on `vp` is an implementation detail inside this exception (ENGINEERING Q-E2 (a) and (c)); the only other exceptions are `pnpm sbom` (I-2) and the mobile toolchain (OR-34, D87).

### 1.1 Pin policy: what "latest" means (D23)

**Status.** RATIFIED (OR-8, OR-9) on 2026-10-01, together with I-3 and I-4. It turns the word "latest" in F1 and F2 into dated rules: the newest release that has passed a cooldown of 7 to 14 days (14 days for a Rust `x.y.0`, 7 days for `vite-plus` and JavaScript packages), or its `x.y.1` patch. The toolchain therefore deliberately runs up to about three weeks behind the newest release. The pnpm major (12.x, OR-28) and the exact dated pins below are not part of this ruling.

**Rust.** `rust-toolchain.toml` carries an exact `channel = "x.y.z"` on every branch, with rustfmt and clippy; builds use `--locked`; release builds set `overflow-checks = true`. A release is eligible when it is x.y.1 or later, or x.y.0 at least 14 days old with no open stable-to-stable regression. Adopt within 7 days of eligibility, so the maximum lag is about three weeks. Reason: 1.96.1, 1.97.1 and 1.98.1 each fixed a miscompilation. Skip list: 1.98.0 (vtable regression, rust-lang/rust#161441). A compiler or std advisory overrides the waiting period on every branch. Release branches inside their security-fix window (D85) keep a per-branch toolchain in a digest-pinned builder image and change it only for such an advisory.

- **Today (2026-10-01): pin 1.98.1 (RATIFIED, D23, OR-8).** Rust 1.99.0 was published on 2026-10-01 (blog post dated that day; `channel-rust-stable.toml` serves it, manifest Last-Modified 12:38 UTC, after a 404 at about 10:10 UTC). The pin stays 1.98.1 because of the rule above (x.y.0 plus 14 days), not because the channel lacks 1.99.0. Adopt 1.99.x between 2026-10-15 (1.99.0 plus 14 days) and 2026-10-22, or at 1.99.1 if that comes first, once debug and release-mode CI pass. This adoption follows mechanically from the ratified pin policy (D23); it needs no separate owner ruling, and none was given. **Status 2026-10-27:** `rust-toolchain.toml` pins 1.99.0, which the rule made eligible from 2026-10-15. 1.99 turns incremental compilation off when `CI` is set. Expect 1.100.0 about 2026-11-12 (eligible 2026-11-26, or at 1.100.1); it brings `wasm32-wasip3` to stable (Tier 3 in 1.99.0; promoted to Tier 2 by rust#161940, milestone 1.100.0).
- **Re-check:** a weekly Renovate toolchain group, a nightly beta canary, and a manual look every six-week release Thursday.

**Vite+.** `vite-plus` is a project-local dependency at an exact version (no range). The exact pnpm is pinned with `vp env pin pnpm@<x.y.z> --target package-manager` (without `--target`, Vite+ 1.0 writes `devEngines.packageManager` instead of the top-level `packageManager` field that the CI guardrails expect), Node in `.node-version`, and `pnpm-lock.yaml` is the only JavaScript lockfile committed (the Rust `Cargo.lock` is committed as well); CI runs `vp install --frozen-lockfile` with `voidzero-dev/setup-vp` at an exact release or SHA (never `v1`) or a digest-pinned image. "Latest Vite+" means the newest `vite-plus` that is at least 7 days old, or its `x.y.1` patch, and passes: the exit drill, a LICENSE/NOTICE diff, exactly one Vite and one Vitest copy (`vp why`), and the test suite. One Renovate group moves `vite-plus`, the `vite` alias to `@voidzero-dev/vite-plus-core` and the `vitest` pin in lockstep.

- **Today (RATIFIED, D23, OR-8):** `vite-plus` 1.0.0 (2026-09-28, MIT) becomes eligible 2026-10-05. Its bundle is the toolchain of record: Vite 8.3.1, Rolldown 1.2.11, Vitest 5.0.1, Oxlint 1.85.0, oxlint-tsgolint 7.0.2003, Oxfmt 0.70.0, tsdown 0.23.0. Upstream is already newer (Vite 8.3.2, Rolldown 1.2.12, Vitest 5.0.3, Oxlint 1.86.0, Oxfmt 0.71.0); that lag is accepted and the bundle is not overridden piecemeal. Exception: a published advisory in a bundled component with no Vite+ release within 7 days permits a logged, temporary override.
- **Node:** 24 LTS now (Status 2026-10-27: `.node-version` already says v26, while the `Dockerfile` web stage still uses `node:24-slim`); move to Node 26 (LTS from 2026-10-28) by 0.2 (2027-02-02) and before the 1.0 branch is cut. Node 24 ends 2028-04-30.
- **pnpm version:** one exact `x.y.z` (never a range or dist-tag), pinned with `vp env pin pnpm@<x.y.z> --target package-manager`, under the same 7-day age rule; the owner ratified the 12.x line (OR-28). Registry state 2026-10-01 14:07 UTC: `latest` and `latest-12` = 12.8.1, `next-12` = 12.8.2, `latest-11` = 11.28.2, `next-11` = 11.28.3. pnpm 12 is the Rust port and the current major; pnpm 11 is the JS line and is still released. The six workspace keys of the settings bullet below were read without warning by pnpm 12.8.1 (checked 2026-10-01, a parse check only); behavioural verification of the six keys is still on 11.x only, so verifying them on 12.x is open engineering work. Under the 7-day rule the newest eligible version today is 12.6.0 or 11.27.1; 12.8.1 and 11.28.2 become eligible on 2026-10-05. **RATIFIED (OR-28, 2026-10-27): the 12.x line; `package.json` pins `pnpm@12.6.0`.**
- **pnpm settings:** `minimumReleaseAge` of at least 7 days (strict), `trustPolicy: no-downgrade`, `blockExoticSubdeps`, `strictDepBuilds` with an `allowBuilds` allow-list, and a scanner that reads both YAML documents of `pnpm-lock.yaml`. **Status 2026-10-27:** until the Expo spike `pnpm-workspace.yaml` held only `packages:`, so nothing enforced the cooldown (pnpm's own default is 1 day and not strict). It now sets `minimumReleaseAge: 10080` (minutes) and `minimumReleaseAgeStrict: true`, the key names of pnpm 12.6.0. Checked on 12.6.0: `vp add expo@57.0.31` (published 2026-10-21) is refused with a message that names the cutoff and `minimumReleaseAgeExclude`; a lock re-resolved under the setting passed an independent check of the publish time of all 780 locked package versions against the npm registry (none younger than 7 days, where the lock before had 15). No exclusion was needed. **Update 2026-10-28:** the file also sets `allowBuilds` with one reviewed entry, `esbuild: false` (a refusal: esbuild is a dependency of Storybook, `web/apps/storybook`, and its install script only swaps its launcher for the platform binary, which comes as an optional dependency and works without it; the comment in the file says so). `trustPolicy`, `blockExoticSubdeps` and `strictDepBuilds` are still not set in the file; setting them is separate engineering work.
- **Exit drill (RATIFIED exception, I-4, OR-9):** a nightly job builds a throwaway copy of the tree with plain Vite 8, Vitest 5, Oxlint and Oxfmt, outside `vp`; a human reviews it quarterly. The copy never ships. The exception is recorded, so the drill is BINDING (D15, ruling 26); it is one of the three named exceptions to "`vp` only" (the others are `pnpm sbom`, I-2, and the mobile toolchain, OR-34, D87). Tripwires that reopen the Vite+ risk (not the constraint): a licence or NOTICE change; remote cache or core `vp` features behind a paid service; a 1.x minor that breaks a CLI flag or config key; plan issue #2405 slipping a quarter.

**Other dependencies.** Versions live in `[workspace.dependencies]`. Renovate cooldowns: crates 7 days, JS packages 7 days, GitHub Actions pinned by SHA 3 days; security updates skip the cooldown but are reviewed by hand. cargo-deny blocks PRs; pre-1.0 vendor crates are pinned exactly; wasmtime follows D11.

**Expo and React Native (OR-34, D87).** The app pins one exact Expo SDK. The owner ruled SDK 57 (React Native 0.86) for now (OR-34 e). An upgrade guide dated 2026-10-21 (seen in a search result, page not read) names SDK 58 (React Native 0.88) as current. **Amended 2026-10-28** (the owner's answer to the lead's question about the move, relayed by the setup session; not a quotation): start the app on SDK 58 (React Native 0.88) if its packages pass the 7 day cooldown of D23 when the app's build starts (step 5 of the Mileage slice); otherwise stay on SDK 57 and move at the first clean point. This amends "for now"; the move is no longer left open. The cooldown that decides it is the one under this section (the newest release that has passed 7 to 14 days). **Pinned during the Expo spike of 2026-10-27, superseded on 2026-10-28** (`docs/spec/mobile.md` 8.3; a dated record, no longer the pin): the spike app pinned `expo` 57.0.30, `react-native` 0.86.3, `react` 19.2.3 and the Expo packages and `typescript` 6.0.3 exactly, at versions of the SDK 57 bill of materials (`expo/bundledNativeModules.json` and Expo's versions API) that were at least 7 days old when pnpm resolved them on 2026-10-27; `expo` 57.0.31 and `expo-location` 57.0.22, both published 2026-10-21, then waited for the cooldown. **Pinned since 2026-10-28** (step 5 of the Mileage slice, `docs/spec/mobile.md` 8.7): SDK 58. `web/apps/mileage-app` pins `expo` 58.0.16, `react-native` 0.88.0, `react` 19.3.0 and the Expo packages of the bill of materials of `expo` 58.0.16 exactly, all at least 7 days old when pnpm resolved them; newer 58.x releases wait for the cooldown (`vp run check:sdk:latest` lists them). Because the online `expo install --check` cannot be clean under the cooldown, `vp run check:sdk` runs the offline form against the bill of materials of the installed `expo` (`scripts/check-sdk.mjs`); that gate is a choice of step 5, not a ruling. Moving to a newer release is a dated adoption (a one-line change per package, then a new lock). The cooldown is enforced by `minimumReleaseAge` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (see the pnpm settings above).

## 2. Decision register

### 2.1 Summary

| ID | Title | Status | Decision in one line |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Licence, contributor terms, publisher | RATIFIED (OR-1, OR-10, OR-18) | Apache-2.0 for the whole project; DCO, no CLA; SpaceCorps is publisher, copyright holder and trademark holder |
| D2 | Open boundary: the software is free, no paid list | RATIFIED (OR-2, OR-22) | No proprietary layer, no paid list, no plans for the software; the one planned paid thing is a hosting service of SpaceCorps (planned, not offered); other services are separate and never restrict the software |
| D3 | Name and trademark | RATIFIED (OR-5) | "obop" is a working name; clearance first, else a screened fallback (the repository was opened on 2026-10-26, which overtook the privacy half of OR-5) |
| D4 | First market, ICP, MVP | RATIFIED for the country (OR-7); ICP and T0 list PROPOSED | Germany first; France then Poland (Proposed); ICP 5-50 person services and light trade (Proposed) |
| D5 | Funding and hiring gates | SUPERSEDED by D84 | Was: gated plan, at most 17 FTE before EUR 1M ARR |
| D6 | Tenancy unit | BINDING | One PostgreSQL database per business; cells of at most 250 for operators |
| D7 | Realtime and queue signalling | BINDING | No NOTIFY in business transactions; outbox plus coalescing notifier |
| D8 | PostgreSQL floor | BINDING | 17 and 18 supported, 18 is the reference |
| D9 | Keys and money | BINDING | App-generated UUIDv7; minor-unit bigint ledger money; no floats |
| D10 | Authorization algebra | BINDING | Odoo-20 algebra, default-deny, RLS as backstop |
| D11 | Extension and scripting model | BINDING | Five code tiers, CEL only in data, a pinned wasmtime LTS, no third-party UI in the main realm |
| D12 | Expression storage | PROPOSED | Domains as JSON AST; all else CEL source plus compiled form |
| D13 | Offline scope | PROPOSED | 1.0 at Odoo-19 parity for the apps shipped by then; POS capture with `pos` (W3); queued writes later |
| D14 | Storefront rendering | PROPOSED | Rust-rendered blocks plus Vite islands; no Node in production |
| D15 | Frontend stack, Vite+ risk | BINDING (the exit-drill exception is RATIFIED, I-4, OR-9) | React 19 and TypeScript 7 on pinned Vite+ behind one config factory |
| D16 | Release, LTS, deprecation | SUPERSEDED by D85 | Was: two series a year, every fourth a 5-year LTS; 24-month deprecation floor |
| D17 | Compliance operating model | RATIFIED (OR-2, OR-10, OR-11); CRA role with counsel (Q5) | Open packs, no operated or warranted services; SpaceCorps as publisher; signed pack channel |
| D18 | AI posture | BINDING | BYOK, no hosted credits, one typed command surface, agents are principals, drafts not posts |
| D19 | Payroll strategy | BINDING | Engine in core, packs, provider connectors; no filing in core; no payroll service |
| D20 | Report and PDF engine | PROPOSED | Typst only in W1-W2 |
| D21 | Performance claims | BINDING | No multiple over Odoo until a harness exists |
| D22 | Odoo-compatibility surface | BINDING | Domain syntax, external ids and importer; no XML-RPC |
| D23 | Pin policy | RATIFIED (OR-8, with I-3, I-4) | Section 1.1 |
| D24 | Canonical vocabulary | BINDING; the renames and the X and L prefixes RATIFIED (OR-14); T0-T3 labels PROPOSED (D25) | Section 4 |
| D25 | Module tiers | PROPOSED (T0 list, Q8); the W1 cut is D86 (RATIFIED, OR-13, OR-15) | T0-T3 with wave placement (section 5) |
| D26 | Release waves and calendar | RATIFIED (OR-13) for the German pack date; the wave windows and the 1.0 target RATIFIED as kept (reading of OR-13 in D26); other dates PROPOSED | W0-W4; one calendar (section 5) |
| D27 | Integration contract | BINDING | Ports, TxEvents, outbox, dependency law |
| D28 | Kernel model and group structure | BINDING | Flat company, branch, ownership edges |
| D29 | Registry, ORM, Rust stack | BINDING | Metadata-first registry; in-house ORM on sqlx and sea-query |
| D30 | Database-enforced integrity | BINDING | Balance, immutability, locks, numbering in PostgreSQL |
| D31 | Public API and versioning | BINDING | Generated REST, MCP, dated version header, free and ungated |
| D32 | Extension packaging and trust | BINDING (trust levels amended by OR-3, D81) | One `obop.toml`, signed OCI artefacts, trust levels private, community, verified, core; sideloading |
| D33 | Security invariants | BINDING | The R18 P0 list |
| D34 | i18n and localisation mechanics | BINDING | PO plus ICU MessageFormat 1; signed effective-dated packs |
| D35 | UI architecture, accessibility | BINDING | Versioned JSON views; WCAG 2.2 AA is a release gate |
| D36 | Quality and release engineering | BINDING | Template-database tests, migration chain tests, generated IDOR matrix |
| D37 | Runtime topology and edge store | BINDING | One binary with roles; SQLite only as fixed-schema edge store |
| D38 | Docs, disclosure and legal pack | BINDING | VitePress in Vite+; equal-time advisories; export everywhere; Apache-2.0 warranty disclaimer |
| D39 | Collaboration and messaging services | BINDING | First-party chatter, OAuth-only mail gateway, honest e-signature |
| D40 | Mask-and-restore at the command boundary | PROPOSED | Per-principal masking by data class on every read surface; reversible tokens restored server-side on write |
| D41 | Agent-ready module contract | PROPOSED | Every command and field carries class, permission and description; REST, MCP and CLI are generated; install gate enforces it |
| D42 | Agent safety timing | PROPOSED | Agent permissions, plan/approve policy and masking ship in W1 with MCP; AI gateway stays W3 |
| D43 | Hosting providers | PROPOSED | Provider-neutral `HostingTarget` port; self-host and Sliplane are reference adapters |
| D44 | Storage backends: SQLite locally, PostgreSQL for deployments | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16; supersedes the "SQLite is never the domain database" part of D37 |
| D45 | Installable declarative packages and the instance admin | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16: admins install X1 `obop.toml` packages from upload or marketplace at runtime; `platform.admin` holds every group |
| D46 | Registry-driven backoffice with floating panels and a Customize app | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16: the UI renders from `/api/meta`; Odoo concepts (app launcher, control panel, list and kanban, form with status bar) in floating panels |
| D47 | Typed fields | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16: fields carry a kind (text, longtext, number, money, date, bool, select, ref) and a required flag; the server validates every write and `/api/meta` exposes them |
| D48 | Governed fields and implicit transitions | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16: a select field that an action moves can only change through that action's group and approval, also when set through `update`; `create` starts at the first option |
| D49 | Delete and server-side search | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-16: generated `delete` (approval for agents, refused while referenced) and `search`; `list` takes filter, order, limit, offset, refused on fields the caller cannot read |
| D51 | Hosted MCP | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: every instance serves MCP over Streamable HTTP at `/mcp` (handshake revisions 2025-03-26 to 2025-11-25) with the REST bearer tokens, sessions that hold the masking vault, and discovery documents; stdio and HTTP share one implementation; 2026-07-28 is not built; the OAuth sign-in came with D64 |
| D52 | Declarative translations | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: packages carry `[labels.<lang>]` tables for module, model, field, option and action names and descriptions; `/api/meta` delivers them; the bundled packages and built-in modules ship machine-drafted de, fr, pl, fi, sv, es |
| D53 | Two-factor authentication | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: RFC 6238 TOTP for people, enforced for administrators by default, secrets sealed under a server key, single-use recovery codes, an append-only `security_event` log; agent tokens are unaffected and cannot manage it; WebAuthn passkeys came with D65; SMS is not built |
| D54 | Connectors and compliant data flows | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: a built-in `connectors` app; every flow in or out passes one policy gate (recorded legal basis, field allowlist, treatment by data class, caps, log without payload); one hardened egress client; signed webhooks; OAuth2 with PKCE; user-owned connections with withdrawable consent; a generated processing register. Supports GDPR compliance; it is not legal advice or a certification |
| D55 | Module metadata, core version and versioned dependencies | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: one core version (0.2.0), reported everywhere; every module declares its version, licence, author, core requirement and dependencies with semver ranges; installs resolve dependencies into one confirmed, atomic plan; uninstalls and upgrades that would break a dependent are blocked; the Modules page shows all of it |
| D56 | Action effects and assertions | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: an action may declare `assert` conditions and effects (create, update, adjust, set_state) that run in the same transaction as its transition, with the caller's permissions, all or nothing; the language is declarative, bounded and checked at install |
| D57 | Computed fields, uniqueness and sequences | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: fields computed on read from a tiny integer expression language and child aggregates; `unique` and `unique_with` enforced by a checked write and a database index; `sequence` fields numbered from transactional counters, gapless on request |
| D58 | Row-level access | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: `[model.access]` rules (own, via, public_field, shared_with, see_all) enforced in every read and write path; an invisible record is a missing record |
| D59 | Operations and durability | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: several server processes may share one PostgreSQL database (sessions, MCP sessions, plans, rate limits, the write lock, the forum and status data and the connector queue live in the database); a server key ring with rotation (`OBOP_SECRET_KEY_PREVIOUS`, `obop secrets rotate`); consistent logical backups with a checked restore and a drill; a schema version with ordered, idempotent, transactional migration steps (`obop migrate`); a published image and a rolling instance upgrade; audited security settings, session and password management and account disable |
| D60 | Blobs and images | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: field kinds `image` and `file` hold the SHA-256 id of a stored blob; content-addressed storage behind a `Blobs` trait (directory, database, memory; an object store is one more byte backend); uploads are sniffed, limited, re-encoded and stripped of metadata; SVG is refused; reads follow the owning record's permissions; the public site serves only blobs held by published records through declared fields |
| D61 | Shop stock, discount codes and taxes | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: checkout takes stock, counts discount uses and creates the order in one atomic batch (`Backend::apply_checked`); codes are checked on the server with one generic refusal and rate-limited failures; taxes are computed per line in exact integers, included or added, by country and category; totals come only from server records |
| D62 | Payments | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-17: a `PaymentProvider` trait with manual (bank transfer), Stripe Checkout and a local demo; the browser is never believed (the server asks the provider, webhooks are verified, deduplicated and cross-checked against the stored amount); every state change is a compare-and-set; refunds run inside the order action, provider first; not-paid orders give stock and code uses back once; no card data, no email receipts, no VAT/OSS engine |
| D64 | OAuth sign-in for MCP clients | BINDING; the amendment of 2026-10-27 (a "mobile" client class, D87) is built (2026-10-28) and stays PROPOSED until the owner rules on the design of D87 | Owner-directed 2026-10-19: every instance is its own OAuth 2.1 authorization server for `/mcp` (RFC 9728 and RFC 8414 metadata, dynamic registration of public clients, authorization code with mandatory PKCE S256 and an RFC 8707 resource, rotating single-use refresh tokens with reuse detection, revocation); a server-rendered sign-in and consent page where the person narrows the groups and picks an expiry; access tokens are the scoped agent tokens of D18, one hour, bound to the `/mcp` audience; connected apps are listed and revoked in Customize, Security; not yet tried against the real Claude apps. Amendment (built 2026-10-28): a client that registers as class `mobile` gets a one hour token for `/api` that is the person narrowed to the groups handed over (never an administrative one), and a `device` record per sign-in that the person lists, renames and revokes |
| D65 | Passkeys | BINDING | Owner-directed 2026-10-19: WebAuthn passkeys as a second factor that satisfies the D53 policy (alone or beside an authenticator app) and, when the person allows it, as a passwordless sign-in with a user-verifying passkey; registration and authentication ceremonies written in Rust over `ring` (ES256, EdDSA, RS256; attestation `none` only), challenges single use and two minutes in the shared store, sign-counter clone detection, a credential list with names and last use, adding, renaming and removing with the password again, the last second factor kept when the policy requires one, the break-glass reset clears passkeys, the same sign-in on the OAuth pages with one script allowed by hash; not tried on real authenticators |
| D66 | TLS to PostgreSQL | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-24: `sslmode=disable|prefer|require|verify-ca|verify-full` and `sslrootcert` in the database URL, rustls with no system OpenSSL, applied to every PostgreSQL connection through one connector; a URL without `sslmode` stays plain; `require` with a root certificate is checked like `verify-ca`; not tried against managed services or poolers |
| D67 | Health and metrics endpoints | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-24: `/healthz` (process), `/readyz` (database ping and compatible schema, 503 with a fixed short reason), `/metrics` (Prometheus text) on every instance; `/metrics` is closed unless `OBOP_METRICS_TOKEN` is set (bearer) or the bind is loopback and the caller is a direct loopback peer; no per-path or per-user labels; not tried against a real scraper or proxy |
| D68 | Allowed origins (CORS) | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-24: `OBOP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is a validated list of exact origins (https, or loopback http; no wildcards, refused at start-up); off by default; for `/api` only: allowed origins get their own origin back, `Vary: Origin`, a preflight with fixed methods and headers and a 600 s max-age, never credentials; other origins get nothing; `/mcp` shares the list; OAuth consent pages unchanged; not tried in a real browser |
| D69 | People administration | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-24 (batch 1): create people, edit their permission groups (checked against the groups the installed modules declare; live sessions follow at the next request; not oneself, not the last administrator), and one offboarding action that disables an account and ends for good its sessions, agent tokens, connected apps and passkey proofs (enabling later revives nothing); REST under `/api/auth/users`, `user_account` commands that agents can only propose, `obop admin users|groups|offboard|disable|enable|import`, the People panel; everything in the security log with before and after; the CSV import checks every row first and writes temporary passwords once or to a 0600 file; not tried with several PostgreSQL processes or the real Claude apps |
| D70 | Bulk create and CSV import | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-24 (batch 1): a generated `bulk_create` on every model (REST, MCP, `obop import`, an Import button in the list toolbar): up to 1,000 rows as a list or as CSV text, every row through the very checks of `create`, one `apply_checked` batch under the write lock, all or nothing by default or `partial` with per-row errors, `dry_run`, an `idempotency_key` whose answer is stored in the same transaction for 24 hours, a rate limit (`OBOP_BULK_PER_MINUTE`, default 12), a strict CSV reader (UTF-8, BOM, `,` or `;`, 1 MiB); whole numbers only, no id remapping, no images or files; not tried against several PostgreSQL processes or 1,000-row calls over a network database |
| D72 | Decimal and datetime field kinds | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25 (storage foundations, step 1): `decimal` (declared `precision` up to 38 and `scale` 0 to 12, an exact `i128` value, JSON form a canonical string with exactly `scale` fractional digits, never a number; strict parser; exact filters, order, `sum`, `min`, `max`, `avg`; exact `+ - *` and rounded `/` in expressions; `round` is the import path's rounding) and `datetime` (an instant with microsecond precision, RFC 3339 with an offset in, UTC `...ffffffZ` out, always six digits); a pinned IANA database (`jiff`, version shown in `/api/meta`), the platform zone `OBOP_ZONE` (default UTC), day, Monday-week, ISO-week, month and year buckets in a declared zone for filters and aggregates, typed `$now` and `$today` in effects; a changed `scale` or smaller `precision` of a decimal needs a `reformat_field` migration step; backoffice (lists, forms, filters, sorting, import and the module builder; not the pivot, graph, kanban, calendar and dashboard views), CLI, REST, MCP and OpenAPI; no retype, no currency, no instant arithmetic |
| D73 | Backup, restore and migration gates | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25 (step 1, storage foundations): a migration ledger (`obop_migration`, one row per step in the step's own transaction, SHA-256 of the step definition; a step edited after it ran stops the server; databases from before it are adopted) with a DDL cost classifier, policy and window rule prepared for D77; migration gates (a verified backup under 24 hours old, a stored plan, room on the disk, a signed rehearsal receipt, a restore point; strict on PostgreSQL, warnings elsewhere; a backup before data-rewriting module updates and before auto-migrate; rollback by restore with a recorded reason); backup format 2 (ChaCha20-Poly1305 in chunks under a per-backup key wrapped by `OBOP_BACKUP_KEY` or the server key, a hash chain and an HMAC over the manifest, `restore_key_required` and exit 3, format 1 still restores); scheduled backups with an off-host copy proved by reading it back and retention (35 daily, 12 monthly); restore drills that digest every restored record; `obop storage check|attest` for WAL archiving, TLS, evidence and encryption at rest, and docs/RUNBOOK-RECOVERY.md; asymmetric keys, an S3 sink, the DDL executor and a managed-service trial are not delivered |
| D74 | Connection pool for PostgreSQL | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25 (step 1, storage foundations), revised after seven adversarial review rounds: a pool written in `obop-storage` replaces the one connection behind a lock (main pool of 8 by default and at most 64, a lock pool of at most 8 that does not count against it, a blob pool); a 2 s acquire timeout and a bounded wait queue refuse the surplus; time limits by command class; scans and exports in one repeatable-read snapshot on one connection under a lane cap, so a long read blocks nobody; recycling, a health check and one repeat of a read on a dead connection; atomic batches take advisory locks on at most 128 stripes, in ascending order, and deadlocks are rerun; **never a silent partial failure: a request that hits a storage fault is answered 503 with `Retry-After` (`storage_busy`, `storage_unavailable`, `storage_timeout`) and `applied`: `none` (nothing was changed, a guard refuses every change after the first refusal), `unknown` (`storage_unavailable`: a change in flight may or may not have been applied) or `partial` (a change committed and a step the request needed was refused after trying again); only a request that lost something optional after its commit keeps its own answer**; a command that names its own `--db` address is a direct connection and ignores the pooler variables; PgBouncer in transaction mode needs `OBOP_DB_POOLER=transaction` and a direct `OBOP_LOCK_DATABASE_URL` (start-up refuses it otherwise); pool series on `/metrics`; not tried against managed services, Linux or a silent network partition |
| D75 | System fields, row version and record history | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25/26 (item 1, storage foundations, second wave): six reserved keys (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by`, `updated_by`, `row_version`, `sys_backfilled`) in the document of every tracked model, stamped by one planner that memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL share (a write that changes nothing leaves no trace; one version per record per transaction); one history entry per changed record per transaction in the same transaction (`{b, a, t}`; secrets never stored, sensitive classes sealed), append-only on PostgreSQL; `if_version`, `If-Match` and `ETag` with 412; the generated command `<model>.history`, a feed, tombstones as `delete` entries; reserved actors `system:internal`, `public:anonymous`, `connector:<id>`; installed modules that use a reserved name are grandfathered. Built so far: the install gate, the planner and the history on memory, SQLite and PostgreSQL behind an empty list of tracked models (no model is tracked, writes are byte for byte as before); the commands, the surfaces and the UI are not built (Q44 to Q47) |
| D76 | Query pushdown to the database, with compiled row rules | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25/26 (item 1, storage foundations, second wave): `list` and `search` evaluated in PostgreSQL by one pure emitter from a registry-free IR, the D58 row rules compiled into the same `WHERE` (default-deny), four strategies (push, over-fetch, finish, the in-memory oracle), text order by eight deterministic ICU collations, a 3 s rule with `storage_timeout` and a `storage_event` log, the switch `OBOP_QUERY_PUSHDOWN` (on, off, shadow), and a differential test against the in-memory evaluator as the proof; amends D49 and one sentence of D58 once built. Built so far: `keyexpr.rs`, the one writer of typed SQL expressions, proven equal to the in-memory evaluator on PostgreSQL (404,580 comparisons, 0 differences), the member tokenizer, the refusal of NUL in a text filter, and the fix of a hang in a non-administrator's aggregate; no query is pushed down yet (Q48 to Q51) |
| D77 | Database-enforced immutability and PostgreSQL role separation | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25/26 (item 1, storage foundations, second wave): `[model.guard]` compiled to one JSON spec that a pure Rust check and one generic trigger enforce (a posted record, a lock date, children of a frozen parent, append-only logs), tickets for governed transitions, `data_guard.report` and `data_guard.verify`, 409 `immutable_record`, `state_conflict`, `guard_change_refused`; an owner role, a runtime role without DDL and a backup role, `Ddl::Never`, `--production`, `--owner-db`, the definer doors `obop_guard_register` and `obop_ensure_unique`, a tamper tool; it stops defects and unaudited paths and, with separate roles, a stolen runtime credential, but not a forged ticket for a declared edge. Built so far: the types, the typed refusal (`OBG01`, `OBG02`), the schema parts and `Ddl`, the ticket transport and the error rows; no guard is enforced anywhere and `OBOP_PRODUCTION` is not read (Q52, Q53) |
| D78 | Declared and automatic indexes, keyset pagination and incremental reads | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25/26 (item 1, storage foundations, second wave): `index = true` and `[[model.index]]` (12 per model, 60 per package, 24 with the automatic ones), automatic indexes for references, row-rule fields and governed fields, definition-hashed names, concurrent builds on a direct session by a reconcile worker or the owner, `storage_index`; keyset paging with a sealed 24 h cursor, total modes, an offset cap of 10,000, a refusal of an unbounded call above 20,000 records, id as the default order of a paged call; `updated_since`, `next_since` (65 s overlap) and tombstones from D75; error codes `cursor_expired` (410), `cursor_invalid`, `cursor_key_too_long`, `offset_too_large`, `limit_required`, `use_search` (400); amends D49. Built so far: the member tokenizer, the expression writer, the registry types and the error rows; no index is declared or built and paging is unchanged (Q54 to Q56) |
| D79 | Persons, organisation, grants and scoped row rules | PROPOSED | Owner-directed 2026-10-25/26 (step 2): a person registry (`org_person`: id = account key = principal id, a minted UUIDv7 or an existing account key adopted once; sign-in through a renameable alias; account plus person created in one atomic batch; a governed lifecycle with a resumable `leave`); an organisation as dated data (levels, nodes, effective-dated edges, memberships with one main, external identifiers, a feed v0) served to the engine by one fail-closed `OrgDirectory`; effective-dated, scoped, approved grants of roles (bundles of groups) beside the unchanged direct groups, with one permission epoch written in the same batch as every change and read once per request, counted and four-eyes approvals, delegation, separation of duties, a fresh-session rule and one security log; one closed row-rule grammar on `[model.access]` (the five D58 keys plus `scope`, `related`, `restrict`, separate write, delete and action positions, field read and write conditions, `create = "effects"`, aggregate-only access, agent writes as proposals) kept as data and folded into a declarative `Residual` that D76 can compile; blobs, computed totals and effect children follow row visibility; an install-time proof (lints, waivers, roles); one test kit (independent reference oracles, a generated cross-scope matrix, seeded mutants); not tried with several PostgreSQL processes under load, the real Claude apps, a real directory feed or a directory of hundreds of people |

| D80 | Agentic-first surface | RATIFIED (OR-4, OR-16); grammar and output details PROPOSED (Q22) | Every capability a typed command in CLI, API and MCP; the UI is a client; parity is the definition of done; UI parity is literal, with an owner-approved exemption list for operator-only commands (OR-16) |
| D81 | Free, open plugin registry | RATIFIED (OR-3, OR-17); staging PROPOSED | Search, install (pinned) or copy via the CLI; only OSI-approved-licence plugins are listed; community and verified tiers (verified = verified publisher plus automated gates); no payments |
| D82 | Contribution model | RATIFIED (OR-1, OR-18) | DCO sign-off by the owner on every commit, agents recorded by a Co-Authored-By trailer, no CLA, inbound equals outbound Apache-2.0; counsel confirms the DCO wording |
| D83 | Hosting posture and a free sandbox | RATIFIED (OR-11, OR-20, OR-22); sandbox details PROPOSED (Q34 to Q36) | One free, ephemeral sandbox with sample data only, opened at the public beta (0.5); a paid hosting service on dedicated Sliplane servers is planned, not offered (OR-22); no other hosting for others |
| D84 | Team and funding posture | RATIFIED (OR-6) | Self-funded; owner plus AI agents; no gates; schedule feasibility is a named risk |
| D85 | Support promise and release train | RATIFIED (OR-12) for the promise; cadence PROPOSED | N-1 and 12-month deprecation before 1.0; 2-year security-fix window from the first stable release |
| D86 | W1 scope cut | RATIFIED (OR-13, OR-15) for the cut; the slip order PROPOSED | Smallest honest loop, confirmed; a stated slip order; dates kept |
| D87 | Native apps (React Native clients) | RATIFIED (OR-34) for parts (a) to (e): React Native (Expo) allowed as an obop client, the mobile-toolchain exception, the manual baseline, Transistorsoft not the default, Expo SDK 57 for now, amended on 2026-10-28 to SDK 58 if its packages pass the cooldown when the app's build starts (they did: the app pins SDK 58, docs/spec/mobile.md 8.7); the scope limits and the technical design PROPOSED (Q37 to Q43) | React Native (Expo) apps may be obop clients (OR-34); the proposal limits them to first-party apps and to cases a PWA cannot serve (Proposed), keeps the PWA as the default and starts with Mileage (a `mileage` package and an app that records a trip while the phone is locked); manual start and stop is the baseline; the mobile toolchain is a third named exception to "`vp` only"; that Apple and Google accounts, store listings and publication stay the owner's is Proposed (Q37) |

Ratified on 2026-10-01 (owner rulings OR-1 to OR-21) and 2026-10-27 (OR-22, OR-34): D1, D2, D3, D4 (country), D5 (superseded), D16 (superseded), D17, D23, D24 (the renames and the X and L prefixes, OR-14), D26 (the German date; the windows and the 1.0 target as kept, on the reading in D26), D80 to D86 (the rulings' own content: for D80 literal UI parity and the exemption-list mechanism, OR-16; for D81 the OSI-licence listing rule and the meaning of verified, OR-17; for D82 the owner's sign-off on every commit with agents recorded by trailer, OR-18; for D83 the hosted sandbox, OR-20; for D84 the no-grant-application position, OR-21; for D86 the W1 cut, OR-15), interpretations I-1 to I-4 (I-1 by OR-14), the exit-drill exception of D15, and D87 for parts (a) to (e) of OR-34 (React Native allowed as a client, the mobile-toolchain exception, the manual baseline, Transistorsoft not the default, Expo SDK 57 for now, amended on 2026-10-28 to SDK 58, which the app pins). Q32 (npm-registry publication) is not ruled: OR-19 defers it to W2 with the default of no publication.

Proposed and awaiting the owner: the scope limits of D87 (first-party apps only, only where a PWA cannot do the job, the PWA stays the default, Tauri mobile is not pursued), the product and works-council sign-offs for auto-stop, and the technical design of D87 (the mobile client class of D64, the `device` model, the device outbox of D13, the `mileage` package, the privacy points; Q37 to Q43), D40, D41, D42 and D43 (Q21), the PROPOSED owner-directed records of the build (D66 to D70, D72 to D79), D4 (ICP, the T0 list, France and Poland), D12, D13, D14, D20, D25 (T0 list, Q8), the T0-T3 labels of D24, the staging and the remaining admission details of D81 (Q24, Q25), the release cadence and the deprecation window after 1.0 in D85 (Q28), the sandbox details of D83 (terms and data policy, host and cost ceiling, abuse and screening approach: Q34 to Q36), the CLI grammar and output conventions of D80 (Q22) and the owner's approval of each entry on the exemption list (OR-16; the entries of spec/cli.md 1.1 are proposals until approved), the slip order, checkpoints and landing waves of D86 (Q8), the npm-registry publication of Q32 (decide at W2, OR-19; default: none), and the remaining dates of D26 (the 0.x releases and the FR and PL pack dates).

### 2.2 Records

#### D1 Core licence, contributor terms, publisher
**Status** RATIFIED (OR-1, OR-10, OR-18). **Reversibility** one-way door (once public, the licence cannot be withdrawn from what was released). **Owner action** none; counsel confirms the DCO wording (Q16).

**Decision.** The whole project is Apache-2.0: the core, every first-party app, the SDKs, WIT worlds, the UI SDK, templates and documentation. There is no copyleft layer, no Application Exception, no dual licence and no `ee` tree. `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` ("Copyright 2026 SpaceCorps contributors") are at the repository root, and every crate, bundled package and JavaScript manifest declares the SPDX identifier `Apache-2.0`. Contributions carry a DCO sign-off; there is no CLA (D82): the owner signs off every commit and agents are recorded with a `Co-Authored-By` trailer. SpaceCorps, the owner's GitHub organisation and brand, is the publisher, the copyright holder and the trademark holder; "steward" is reserved for a foundation that may replace it, and counsel can move the publisher role to one later. The CRA manufacturer or steward analysis for a free project with SpaceCorps as publisher goes to counsel (Q5); counsel also has in view the sample-data sandbox (OR-20, D83) and the planned hosting service (OR-22).

**Why.** The owner's ruling: completely free to use and to self-host, with no proprietary layer (D2). A permissive licence is the plainest statement of that promise and removes the extension-boundary, CLA and dual-licence questions. **Superseded alternative.** AGPL-3.0-or-later core with an s7 Application Exception, Apache-2.0 SDKs, a commercial `ee` tree, a licence-grant CLA with an "OSI licence forever" pledge, and a company plus an EU non-profit steward with a veto (former D1, R17 s11-12, R24 s8, R26 C2-C3). **Rejected.** FSL, BUSL, SSPL, ELv2 (not OSI, not free in the owner's sense). **Consequences.** The repository `SpaceCorps/obop` is public (the owner opened it on 2026-10-26, which overtook the gate G0 of D26 and the privacy half of OR-5). Anyone may fork, host or embed obop, including inside proprietary products; the only lever against a confusing fork or host is the trademark (Q26). There is no CLA, so contributors keep their copyright and no unilateral relicensing is planned. In-process third-party Rust stays unsupported for security reasons, no longer for licence reasons (D11). The dependency allow-list admits permissive and MPL-2.0 dependencies and excludes GPL, AGPL, SSPL, BUSL, ELv2 and other non-OSI licences from anything shipped (Proposed detail; GPLv2-only and LGPL cases go to counsel, CN-08). Of the nine counsel items of R17 s12, the exception, CLA, mixed-licence and patent-pledge items fall away; the name (Q2), the CRA role (Q5), the publisher and trademark matters (Q16, Q26) and dependency licences remain. Nothing here is legal advice. **Evidence.** OR-1, OR-10, OR-18; R17 s11-12, R24 s8 and R26 C2-C3 (superseded), R05.

#### D2 Open boundary: the software is free, no paid list
**Status** RATIFIED (OR-2, OR-22). **Reversibility** one-way door for the software (a paid layer added later would break the promise). **Owner action** none; the commercial set-up of the hosting service is open (OR-22).

**Decision.** obop is "completely free to use, you can self host" (owner). There is no proprietary paid software at all: no open-core, no enterprise layer, no closed or ratcheting paid list, no paid plans for the software, no per-seat or per-organisation pricing, no paid partner programme and no marketplace commission. The one planned paid thing is a hosting service that SpaceCorps itself runs for businesses that would rather not run a server: a dedicated Sliplane server at three times the provider's list price, rounded up to a price ending in 9, extra storage extra (OR-22). It is separate from the software and restricts nothing in it, and it is planned, not offered or charged (no billing, terms or processing agreement exist). Any other future service (support, certification) would also be separate and must never restrict the software; introducing one needs a published amendment to this record and to section 6 (Proposed).

**Why.** The owner's ruling. Odoo's gated accounting and API are the main grievance, and with nothing gated no open/paid line can move. **Superseded alternative.** Everything open with paid operation and assurance, a closed ratcheting paid list, `ee` conversion after 24 months, per-organisation plans (Free, EUR 49, EUR 249, from EUR 899) and a marketplace take rate of 0% to 15% (former D2, R26 A2-A3, R25 B7, R24 s10, R17 s11.2). **Rejected.** Any paid tier or paid feature of the software. OR-2 of 2026-10-01 also rejected operation-only paid services; OR-22 of 2026-10-27 then allowed one, the hosting service. **Consequences.** Every former paid-list capability that is software (native multi-IdP SAML, full SCIM, WORM audit publication, SIEM streaming, cloud-KMS and HSM connectors, customer-managed keys, fleet disaster recovery, the supported air-gap bundle) is an ordinary backlog item: not withheld, not promised (section 6). Apart from hosting, what needed an operator (hosted gateways, hosted AI credits, the Assurance and payroll services, support levels, white-label, certification) is not built or sold at all (D83). R26's pricing, plan, ARPA, partner-economics and marketplace material is historical. Q3, Q13 and Q19 are closed. **Evidence.** OR-2, OR-22; R26 A2-A3, R25 B7, R24 s10 and R17 s11.2 (all superseded), R03, R04.

#### D3 Name and trademark
**Status** RATIFIED (OR-5); the trademark policy is PROPOSED (Q26). **Reversibility** one-way door once announced or filed. **Owner action** the counsel opinion (Q2).

**Decision.** "obop" stays the working name; clearance comes first. Counsel's opinion is due by 2026-12-15, else a screened fallback is adopted. No announcement, spend or filing until counsel clears EUIPO, UKIPO, WIPO, DPMA and INPI in classes 9, 35, 42 (and 41, 45) and opines on OBOPP (pending, class 35) and OBOL (two live, classes 9 and 42). The owner opened the repository `SpaceCorps/obop` on 2026-10-26, before clearance, so the gate G0 of D26 and the privacy half of OR-5 are overtaken; the clearance opinion, the name and the publisher matters stay open (Q2, Q16). Reserve the crate, PyPI, GitHub organisation variant, GHCR and Matrix handles now. The npm registry name `obop` is taken; that is a clearance fact only, because obop reserves no npm scope (Q32). Keep screened fallbacks (kernelbiz, monobiz, mercora). SpaceCorps is the trademark holder and files in its own name (OR-10); counsel can move the marks to a foundation later.

**Why.** Clearance risk is medium; US intent-to-use marks cannot be assigned early, which matters if the marks later move to a foundation. **Superseded alternative.** Filing in the name of an EU non-profit steward (former D3). **Rejected.** Rename now; proceed and file. **Consequences.** A public repository is an announcement; it was opened on 2026-10-26, before counsel's opinion, so a rename after the opinion (Q2) now touches public artefacts (the repository, crates, images). Apache-2.0 grants no trademark rights, so a trademark policy for forks, hosts and plugin names is still needed (Q26). **Evidence.** OR-5, OR-10; R28 s2, A.5.

#### D4 First market, ICP and MVP shape
**Status** RATIFIED for the country (OR-7); the ICP, the T0 app list and the order of France and Poland are PROPOSED. **Reversibility** two-way door, but costly (design partners, packs). **Owner action** yes for the Proposed part (Q8).

**Decision.** Germany first for the first compliance pack (RATIFIED; pack stable 2027-09-30).
