Proposed mechanics, not ruled: pre-1.0 minors on first Tuesdays (0.1 2026-12-01 to 0.7 2027-11-02, D26); after 1.0 one stable series a year on the third Tuesday of April (2029-04-17, then 2030-04-16: the Q28 recommendation, used as the planning assumption until the owner answers; the earlier draft's October slots, 2028-10-17 and 2029-10-16, apply only if Q28 keeps two series a year); contract migrations and API removals only at the first release of a series (`N.0`), expand-only in between (D36); the deprecation window after 1.0 stays 12 months; `compat.json` per release; usage telemetry opt-in; a stable-series window of 2 years from its release date. Whether the CRA's five-year minimum support period reaches a free project with SpaceCorps as publisher is a counsel question (Q5); if it does, the window lengthens by law, not by this record.

**Why.** The owner's ruling: a promise a very small team can keep. **Superseded alternative.** Two series a year with every fourth an LTS supported for five years (years 0-2 full, 2-5 security and legal-critical), a monthly patch train, hosted-fleet rings, a 24-month deprecation floor with release-month counting, and an SLA (former D16). **Rejected.** Any promise that needs funding to keep. **Consequences.** "1.0 LTS" becomes "1.0" everywhere; "LTS" is retired as a term. Q7 is closed. With two series a year and a 2-year window, four lines could be live at once; a team this small cannot keep that, which is why Q28 proposes a yearly stable series and the mechanics above plan on it. The ROADMAP 7 release table, the ENGINEERING 4.2-4.6 sections and the ARCHITECTURE risk lines that assumed a "24-month LTS" were conformed to this record; the hosted-fleet rings are operator guidance (ENGINEERING 4.6). **Evidence.** OR-12; R27 C1-C8 and R26 T4 (historical), ENGINEERING 4.

#### D86 W1 scope cut: the smallest honest loop
**Status** RATIFIED (OR-13, OR-15) for the cut: the shape (kernel, invoicing, accounting with tax, German e-invoicing, CRM and sales, basic stock), the in and out calls of section 5.3, the kept dates and the principle that everything else slips first in a stated order; the slip order, its checkpoints and the landing wave of each displaced item (except the services variant, W3) are PROPOSED. **Reversibility** two-way door. **Owner action** Q8 (confirm the slip order).

**Decision.** W1 (2027-01-01 to 2027-09-30) is cut to the smallest honest loop: the kernel (T0), invoicing, accounting with tax, German e-invoicing and `l10n_de`, CRM and sales, and basic stock. Dates do not move; scope does. The owner confirmed the cut as it stands in section 5.3 (OR-15): in are the kernel, invoicing, accounting with tax, German e-invoicing (XRechnung and Factur-X by email or file), CRM and sales, one-warehouse stock, CLI, REST and MCP, and a thin UI; out of W1 are purchase orders, the customer portal, Peppol transport, SEPA and Stripe payments, inbound mail and pricing rules; project and timesheet (the services variant) go to W3. The owner pulled nothing back into W1. Section 5.3 holds the necessary-inside calls (what else is strictly necessary inside that loop, each with a one-line justification; RATIFIED by OR-15, except the conditions it states on rows 16 and 21) and the slip order (which item leaves first when time is short, with the wave it lands in; Proposed). Items return in the reverse of their slip order: the last to slip is the first back. The displaced items move to W2 and W3 (section 5.1 and 5.2). Exit criteria are correctness and adoption measures, not customer or revenue counts (section 5.2). D33's P0 and P1 security items and the D30 database-enforced integrity are not cut by this record.

**Why.** The owner's ruling, and ROADMAP 4.5's finding that three items alone used 78% to 96% of W1 capacity at 6 to 10 FTE. **Superseded alternative.** R20 s9.3's seven-app loop, including purchase orders, payments providers, portal, six view types, Peppol through a partner, QuickBooks and the extension platform cut of R24 s11 (26 to 34 person-months) in W1 (former D25 and D26 W1 content). **Rejected.** Moving dates; a hosted on-ramp (R20's hosted Free plan; the sandbox of D83 is not one); keeping R20's list and hoping. **Consequences.** W1 ships with a thinner UI (list, form, kanban, search and the accounting views) and complete CLI, API and MCP surfaces for what it contains (D80); services firms wait for the services variant in W3; a light-trade firm that needs purchase orders waits for W2. With X3 in W3 (2028-07 onward), the Wasm host should start on the wasmtime LTS then current (v60, about 2027-08-20) instead of v48, whose support ends about 2028-08; D11's v48 pin then covers only the internal spike (ROADMAP 9.3 item 6; an ADR amends D11 when X3 starts). Locales: en and de are the W1 commitment; the other target locales of D34 arrive as community translations that pass review (Proposed, an amendment of D34's W1 reading). **Evidence.** OR-13, OR-15; R20 s9.3, R23 D, ROADMAP 4.5.

#### D87 Native apps (React Native clients)
**Status** RATIFIED (OR-34) for parts (a) to (e) of the ruling: React Native (Expo) apps may be clients, the mobile-toolchain exception, manual start and stop as the baseline, Transistorsoft not the default, Expo SDK 57 for now (amended on 2026-10-28: SDK 58 if its packages pass the cooldown when the app's build starts, otherwise SDK 57 until the first clean point; the app pins SDK 58 since 2026-10-28, docs/spec/mobile.md 8.7). The scope limits that came with the proposal (first-party apps only, only where a PWA cannot do the job, the PWA stays the default, Tauri mobile is not pursued), the product and works-council sign-offs for auto-stop, visible recording, the `expo-location` default path and its fallback, the reason given for excluding Transistorsoft and the remark that an optional adapter is not forbidden, the rule that Apple and Google accounts, store listings and publishing are the owner's alone, and the technical design below (the mobile sign-in class, the `device` model, the device outbox, the `mileage` package, the privacy points, the repository layout) are PROPOSED until built. **Reversibility** two-way door while nothing is published (the exception, the library and the SDK can change); once an app is on a store, its application id, signing key and permission declarations are hard to change. **Owner action** yes: Q37 to Q43. The proposal is that Apple and Google developer accounts, store listings and publishing are the owner's alone (Q37).

**Decision.** Owner: "React Native (Expo) allowed as an obop client with the mobile-toolchain exception; manual start and stop as the baseline, auto-stop only after legal sign-off; Transistorsoft excluded as the default; Expo SDK 57 for now." (given in chat to the main session, relayed to the lead session on 2026-10-27; OR-34). *Permission (OR-34 a RATIFIED; the scope limits Proposed).* React Native apps built with Expo are allowed as clients of the same REST and OpenAPI surface that the web back office uses (D31, D80). The proposal limits them to first-party apps and to cases where a PWA cannot do the job, that is where a background capability is needed (Proposed). Recording a trip while the phone is locked is the first such case (from general knowledge, not tested here: a browser page does not keep receiving location updates in the background). Also Proposed: the PWA stays the default client, Tauri 2 stays the shell of POS and desktop (D37), and Tauri mobile is not pursued. An app has no private backdoor: it calls REST and the OpenAPI document, signs in through OAuth (D64) and meets the same checks of the one Dispatcher as every other client; a capability it needs exists first as a typed command in the CLI, the API and MCP (D80). The first app is Mileage: a `mileage` package (trips, points, a review state machine) and an Expo app that keeps recording a trip while the phone is locked or the app is in the background. The specification is [spec/mobile.md](spec/mobile.md). "Native app" in this record means a native mobile app; it is unrelated to the package kind `native` of D32.

*Toolchain (RATIFIED, OR-34 b: "with the mobile-toolchain exception"; the conditions are the exception as proposed and recorded).* Metro, the Expo CLI, Gradle and Xcode are started through `vp env exec` or `vp run` tasks. This is the third named exception to "`vp` only", beside the two of OR-9 (the nightly exit drill, I-4; `pnpm sbom`, I-2). pnpm stays the only package manager and only `pnpm-lock.yaml` is committed; `vp check`, `vp test` and `vp build` still cover every shared TypeScript package (the client, token and trip-logic packages that the app and the web back office share). Proposed: no `ios/` or `android/` directory is checked in (Expo config plugins and `expo prebuild`), native builds run locally, and no dependency on Expo's cloud services is planned for builds or updates. Metro with pnpm's symlinked `node_modules` was open and is now verified by the first spike (`docs/spec/mobile.md` 8.3, 2026-10-27): the default Metro configuration of Expo SDK 57 resolved a workspace package with no extra configuration.

*Recording (RATIFIED, OR-34 c and d).* The baseline is manual: the driver taps to start and taps to stop. Auto-stop, which ends a trip the driver did not end, comes only after legal sign-off (Q41). The Transistorsoft library is not the default. Proposed, with the proposal: the reason is that its release builds need a paid licence key (OR-1, OR-2), and an optional adapter later is not forbidden and must never sit in the default build path.

*Recording design (PROPOSED).* Recording is visible (an Android notification, the iOS indicator). The proposal adds product and works-council sign-off to the owner's legal sign-off for auto-stop, and all three are required first (Q41). Automatic start is not ruled and is out of scope: manual start is the baseline. The default background-location path is `expo-location` with `expo-task-manager` and an Android foreground service, which is free and needs no licence key. An own native module is the fallback if `expo-location` proves unreliable in the field spike.

*Sign-in and devices (PROPOSED; the class and the `device` model were built on 2026-10-28, see the D64 amendment).* The system browser only (RFC 8252), through `obop-oauth`: a client class "mobile" with the `/api` audience (the D64 amendment), PKCE S256, rotating refresh tokens with reuse detection, scopes narrowed the way agent tokens narrow groups; passkeys come with the browser page (D65). The refresh token lives in the platform keystore. A built-in model `device` holds the user, name, platform, app version, push token, created, last seen and revoked; the refresh-token family is bound to the device id, so revoking the device ends the family and its queued commands are refused. Devices are listed in Customize, Security beside the Connected apps table. This is the same kind of revocation that D13 and security-privacy 6.3 promise for POS devices; whether this `device` model and the `pos_device` record are one model is open (spec/mobile.md 15).

*Offline (PROPOSED).* A native app keeps a command outbox in SQLite on the device (the D13 amendment), encrypted at rest with a key in the platform keystore: UUIDv7 `command_id`s, replay through normal domain services, the idempotency key of `bulk_create` (D70: 24 hours, per person, model and key) with unique constraints (D57) behind it, while a replayed `update` simply sets the same values (`if_version`, reserved by D75, is not yet accepted by `update`). The trip's record id is assigned by the server (D70 refuses a supplied id), so the device keeps a `client_trip_id` of its own (spec/mobile.md 5.3). Where the design needs more than the platform has (a state-conditioned write rule, separation of duties, a retryable refusal), spec/mobile.md 5.8 lists it as a required capability. It does not use AsyncStorage or browser storage.

*Location data (PROPOSED; not legal advice).* Location is personal data and a new processing location for the impact assessment. Raw points are minimised (thinned or deleted a stated number of days after a trip is confirmed; the distance and a simplified route stay); no third-party geocoder or map tiles by default (Q39); every read of a route is audited; recording is active only while the driver started it; the server, not the client, computes and validates the distance. A new data class `location` is Proposed (Q40). Retention also covers the encrypted device outbox and the D75 change history of the points. Details are in spec/mobile.md 5.

*Limits (PROPOSED).* No Apple or Google account is used, no store listing is made and nothing is published without the owner (Q37). Tests use synthetic data only. Android comes first because it can be built on the Windows development machine; iOS comes second, when a Mac is available (Xcode runs on macOS only; general knowledge).

*Delivered by the mobile sign-in of 2026-10-28 (docs/spec/mobile.md 8.5):* the "mobile" client class of `obop-oauth` with its redirect rules and the `/api` audience, the `device` model with `device.revoke`, the revocation of devices by a credential change and an offboarding, the Devices table in Customize, Security. *Delivered by the Expo spike of 2026-10-27 (docs/spec/mobile.md 8.3):* `web/packages/trip-core` (then one distance function with tests; the track functions of 8.6 came later) and a first `web/apps/mileage-app` (an Expo SDK 57 app with one screen, since replaced by the app below), built for Android on a Windows machine and run on an emulator, with Metro and pnpm's symlinked `node_modules` verified. *Delivered by the typed client of 2026-10-28 (8.4):* `@obop/client` (types generated from the OpenAPI export, the call and error layer). *Delivered by the Mileage backend of 2026-10-28 (8.6):* the built-in module `crates/modules/mileage` (trips and points, review, distance at `end`); there is no `packages/mileage.toml`. *Delivered by step 5, the Android app (2026-10-28, 8.7):* `web/apps/mileage-app` on Expo SDK 58 with sign-in through the system browser (PKCE; the helper is `web/packages/client/src/oauth.ts`), token storage in the Android Keystore, an encrypted SQLite outbox, a foreground-service location task and the screens Sign in, Permissions, Home, Recording, Trips, Trip detail, Account and Foreign (the trips of another sign-in); `web/packages/ui-native` (React Native components with generated native tokens); and `web/apps/storybook` (the components in the web Storybook through react-native-web). The app was built for Android and run on an Android emulator only. *Not delivered:* a test on a real phone (the acceptance test of spec/mobile.md 9, with the steps of 9.1, is still to be run), anything on iOS, release signing (the build is signed with the debug key), a store listing and the Play declarations, the app's label tables in languages other than English (step 6), push, and auto-stop. Not verified: Apple's documentation (the iOS rules are from general knowledge); Expo's iOS behaviour with `CLBackgroundActivitySession`; whether a foreground service started from a visible app, or a trip recording of hours that a driver starts, avoids the Google Play background-location declaration (the policy text was read and does not say); whether `expo-location` works on Android without Google Play services (Q43); an Android build on Linux or macOS, with a JDK other than 17 or for all four ABIs; the Docker image build with the Expo packages in the workspace; the accessibility class of `expo-secure-store` items read while the phone is locked; the Transistorsoft price; the maintenance of self-hosted Expo update servers; the Expo release dates (seen in a search result). Closed by step 5 (8.7; on an emulator only): how to encrypt an `expo-sqlite` database (the app uses SQLCipher), and the SDK of the Expo documentation read (SDK 58, which the app now pins).

**Why.** A mileage report needs the route while the phone is in a pocket, which a PWA cannot promise (general knowledge), and a native client is the smallest thing that can. React Native with Expo keeps TypeScript, the pnpm workspace and the shared client and token packages the same as the web back office, and `expo-location` needs no paid library. Keeping the app a plain client keeps D80: nothing is app-only. A manual baseline avoids ending a trip the driver did not end, which is a labour-law and data-protection question. **Rejected.** A PWA alone (cannot record in the background; general knowledge); Capacitor (not React Native; a prior mileage app used it and its first real drive exposed seven bugs, so it is evidence and not a precedent); Tauri mobile (maturity unverified, R07 s3.9); Transistorsoft as the default (a paid key in the build path of the first app); AsyncStorage or browser storage for raw points (can be cleared under storage pressure and holds raw GPS unencrypted); an embedded web view for sign-in (RFC 8252); trusting a distance the client computed; automatic trip end in the baseline; third-party geocoding or map tiles by default. **Consequences.** The non-goal "native store apps beyond the PWA and Tauri shells" is replaced in PRODUCT, ux 8, people 8 and ARCHITECTURE 10.5; D13 and D64 each carry an amendment; "exactly two exceptions" becomes three named exceptions in CLAUDE.md and the documents that repeated it (OR-9 still rules what it ruled; OR-34 adds one). The tooling, the layout (`web/packages/client`, `web/packages/tokens`, `web/packages/trip-core`, `web/apps/mileage-app`, `packages/mileage.toml`) and the first slice are in spec/mobile.md 8 and 9; the risks (Android vendor battery limits, Google Play and App Review of background location, GPS that emulators cannot fully test, SDK churn, location-data compliance) are in 13. **Evidence.** OR-34; the design proposal of 2026-10-27 written by the lead session, whose markings (verified, general knowledge, unverified) spec/mobile.md keeps; D13, D15, D23, D31, D35, D37, D54, D64, D65, D70, D72, D75, D80; RFC 8252.

## 3. Contradiction rulings

| # | Topic | Ruling | Authority | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | NOTIFY | Forbidden in business transactions; outbox, in-process wake-up, coalescing notifier on one bus database | R23 DR-1, D7 | BINDING |
| 2 | Tenancy | Database per business only; pool mode not built; the 250-database spike sets the cell size; no Free tier exists (OR-2) | R23 DR-2, D6 | BINDING; pooled mode DEFERRED |
| 3 | PostgreSQL floor | 17 and 18; `requires_pg = 18` for 18-only features | R23 DR-3, D8 | BINDING |
| 4 | Authorization | Odoo-20 algebra, default-deny | R23 DR-4, D10 | BINDING |
| 5 | Scripting in data | CEL only for conditions, modifiers and formulas; render-time templates are a bounded category (D11); no Rhai in W1-W2; cost-limit spike | R24 s3.2, D11 | BINDING |
| 6 | Modifier storage | Domains JSON AST; the rest CEL source plus compiled form | D12 | PROPOSED |
| 7 | Wasmtime LTS | v36 and v48 are LTS; pin v48; next v60 about 2027-08-20; R21's claim is wrong | R24 s3.3, D11 | BINDING |
| 8 | Frontend extension | F1 only for first-party and business-own code; registry plugins are F2 or F3 | R24 s4, D11, D81 | BINDING |
| 9 | Edge datastore | PostgreSQL server; SQLite only as fixed-schema edge store | R23 DR-7, D37 | BINDING |
| 10 | Storefront and Node | No Node in production; build-time only | D14 | PROPOSED |
| 11 | One edition versus paid security | Nothing is paid: every capability and every security outcome is open; no paid list, no ratchet (R26 A2 and R25 B7 superseded) | OR-2, D2 | RATIFIED |
| 12 | CLA | None. Apache-2.0 in and out with a DCO sign-off; no pledge, no steward veto, no investor covenant (R26, R17 superseded) | OR-1, D1, D82 | RATIFIED |
| 13 | Pricing unit | None: no prices, no plans, no price tests (R26 A3 superseded) | OR-2, D2 | RATIFIED |
| 14 | Deprecation window | 12 months before 1.0 (RATIFIED); after 1.0 the 12 months continue unless the owner rules otherwise (PROPOSED); the 24-month floor is withdrawn | OR-12, D85 | RATIFIED before 1.0; PROPOSED after |
| 15 | Timeline | One calendar; public beta, pack stable and 1.0 separated; keep the dates, shrink the scope | OR-13, D26, section 5 | RATIFIED for the German date; the windows and the 1.0 target RATIFIED as kept (reading of OR-13 in D26); other dates PROPOSED |
| 16 | First country | Germany first (RATIFIED); France then Poland (PROPOSED); conditions in D4 | OR-7, R25, D4 | RATIFIED for Germany; PROPOSED for the order after |
| 17 | MVP breadth | D86 cut lines govern W1 (R20 s9.3 superseded); R23 graph is the dependency law | OR-13, OR-15, D86, D25 | RATIFIED cut (OR-15); slip order PROPOSED |
| 18 | Odoo 20 Field Service | Merged into Planning (R14); task subtype versus shift stays open | R14, section 2.3 | DEFERRED |
| 19 | Odoo 20 release and MCP | "September 2026 (release commit 2026-09-17)"; `/mcp` with API-key auth, server actions as tools | R16, R21 | BINDING |

## 4. Canonical vocabulary

| Term | Meaning | Avoid |
|---|---|---|
| obop | Product and project; lower case | OBOP, Obop |
| business | Isolation and deployment unit: one PostgreSQL database, module set, user directory, object-store prefix, backup unit | "tenant", "instance", "workspace" in the data plane |
| tenant | Owner principal in the control plane of whoever operates many businesses; owns one or more businesses; absent from business databases. There is no billing (OR-2) | In schemas, APIs or data-plane code. Renames (RATIFIED, OR-14): `TenantTx` to `BusinessTx`, `TenantRouter` to `BusinessRouter`, `tenant_release_state` to `business_release_state` |
| organisation (org) | Customer-facing name for one business (not a billing unit) | For a legal person (use "legal entity") |
| legal entity | A juridical person, stored as an organisation `party` | "entity" alone |
| company | Ledger-owning scope of one legal entity: books, functional currency, fiscal calendar, journals, sequences, lock dates | For a counterparty or a branch |
| branch | Subdivision of a company without own books | "subsidiary" (that is a company) |
| company group | Holding and subsidiaries, modelled as `ownership_interest` edges | "group" alone |
| permission group | Named bundle of permission rows (Odoo `res.groups`) | "group" alone; "role" outside UI copy |
| permission row, restriction row | Rows of the D10 algebra | "ACL", "record rule", "sudo" |
| party | Person or organisation in the data model | "partner" in data (Odoo's `res.partner`) |
| partner | A third party that implements, hosts or localises obop; there is no partner programme, fee or revenue share (OR-2) | For a contact or customer |
| principal | User, portal user, guest, API client, agent or system | "bot" for agents |
| cell | One PostgreSQL cluster of at most 250 business databases, with PgBouncer and app nodes | |
| control plane, data plane | Fleet and release tooling for whoever operates many businesses (open code, not part of a single-business install), versus business databases and app nodes | "`ee`" |
| module | First-party unit with manifest, `*-api` and implementation crates, installed per business | "addon" |
| app | A module shown as a top-level menu entry | For a package |
| package | Distribution unit in the package registry (`obop.toml`); kinds pack, wasm, remote, native, theme | |
| extension | Any third-party or business-own package | |
| plugin | The owner's word (OR-3) for a package in the registry; use it in CLI commands and user-facing copy, and write "package" or "extension" in architecture and data-model text | "plugin" for an in-process Rust crate or for a first-party module |
| install, copy | The two CLI modes for a plugin (D81): install fetches a signed, version-pinned package; copy puts its source into the user's own instance as business-own code | "fork" |
| trust level | Package level: `private` (business-own), `community`, `verified` (a verified publisher plus automated gates, OR-17), `core` (D32, D81) | "reviewed", "partner" (retired) |
| typed command, command catalogue | One capability as one entry of the registry-generated catalogue, reachable through the CLI, the REST API and MCP (D80) | "endpoint", "RPC", a capability on one surface only |
| parity | The definition of done of D80: the command exists in CLI, API and MCP, and in the UI before the first stable release, except commands on the exemption list (OR-16) | "feature-complete" for a feature that lacks a surface |
| exemption list | The owner-approved list of operator-only commands that lack a UI (CLI-only), each with a written reason and the surfaces it lacks (D80, OR-16); the entries proposed in [spec/cli.md](spec/cli.md) 1.1 are not exemptions until approved | "UI-exempt" or "exempt class" for a command the owner has not approved; a UI-only step as an exemption |
| remote app | Package of kind `remote` (code tier X4) | "app" alone |
| pack | Package of kind `pack` (declarative data, optionally plus code); a localisation pack is a country pack | For a service (none exists) |
| registry | The runtime model registry (`Arc<Registry>`) | For the package registry (say "package registry" or "plugin registry"), crates.io or the npm registry |
| port | A Rust trait in a `*-api` crate implemented by its owner; a "hub port" (`DemandPort`) is a convergence port | A network port, unqualified |
| TxEvent, durable event | In-transaction event; outbox-backed event | "event" alone in architecture text |
| outbox | `outbox_event` plus `outbox_delivery` rows | The edge `command_outbox` (say "edge command outbox") |
| bus database | The one database per cluster hosting the coalescing notifier | "bus" alone |
| edge store | SQLite store of `obop-edge` | A replica of the server schema |
| store hub | The `hub` role serving a shop's tablets | "hub" alone |
| open edition | Everything, under Apache-2.0: one codebase, one edition, no `ee` tree, no paid layer (OR-1, OR-2) | "Community", "Enterprise", "Studio" (Odoo words), "`ee`", "paid list" |
| X1-X5 | Code tiers: pack, CEL, Wasm, remote, native (RATIFIED labels, OR-14) | T1-T5 (R24) |
| F0-F3 | UI extension tiers; write "UI tier F2", never bare, because F1-F6 are the owner constraints | Bare "F2" |
| L1-L3 | Localisation pack maintenance tiers: core team, third-party maintainers, community (RATIFIED labels, OR-14; no payment, OR-2) | T1-T3 (R25); "paid partner" |
| T0-T3 (Proposed, D25) | Module tiers (section 5) | R23's "T0 (21) and T1 (19)" sets; R26 risk ids read "risk T7" |
| W0-W4 (D26) | Release waves (section 5) | "Phase", "wave" for country order, bare "GA" |
| pack stable, public beta, 1.0 | Pack gate "stable"; the 0.5 public pre-release; the first stable release (D85) | "DE GA", "paid beta", "1.0 LTS" |
| series | Release `N.M`; its security-fix window is set by D85 | "LTS" (retired) |
| operator, implementer, publisher, host, steward, manufacturer | Runs an installation; configures it; authors a package (SpaceCorps for obop itself); runs obop for others (SpaceCorps does not today, apart from the sandbox, D83; the planned hosting service of OR-22 would); a foundation that may one day replace SpaceCorps as publisher (OR-10); the CRA role, for counsel (Q5) | "Foundation" or "steward" for SpaceCorps (except inside quoted research) |
| sandbox, local demo | The sandbox is the free, ephemeral, hosted obop that SpaceCorps runs from the public beta, with sample data only (D83, OR-20). The local demo is a seeded instance on the user's machine (`obop dev up`, Proposed: no `dev` command is built yet; `--demo` and the default empty-SQLite demo data exist) | "demo tenant", "trial", "free tier" or "cloud" for the sandbox; "sandbox" for a test copy of a business (say "business clone") |

**Identifier namespaces.** Each prefix has one meaning in this register. Where a companion document or research note uses the same letters for something else, write the qualifier every time ("UI tier F2", "constraint F2", "R26 risk M9", "month M9", "integration rule R5", "research note R05"). Companion documents may define their own families (gates, schedule risks, spikes, goals, open questions, counsel items; the hiring gates HG0-HG3 of ROADMAP are withdrawn with OR-6); those are read as defined there and never cited in this register without the document name.

| Family | Meaning in this register | Defined in |
|---|---|---|
| D1-D87 | Decision records | Section 2 |
| OR-1 to OR-34 | Owner rulings of 2026-10-01 (OR-1 to OR-21) and 2026-10-27 (OR-22 to OR-34) | "Owner rulings (2026-10-01)" and "Owner rulings (2026-10-27)" above |
| Q1-Q56 | Owner questions | Section 8 |
| F1-F6 | FIXED owner constraints | Section 1 |
| I-1 to I-4 | Interpretations of F1 and F2 (RATIFIED) | Section 1 |
| R00-R28 | Research notes (two digits; "R23 DR-1" is decision record 1 of note 23) | Section 9 |
| R1-R7 | Integration rules (one digit) | D27 |
| X1-X5, F0-F3, L1-L3 | Code tiers, UI tiers, pack maintenance tiers | This section |
| T0-T3, W0-W4 | Module tiers, release waves | Section 5 |
| G0 | Public-repository gate (overtaken, OR-26: the repository is public since 2026-10-26) | D26 |
| M0-M36 | Months counted from 2026-10-01 (never R26 risk ids such as M7 or M9) | Section 0 |
| P0-P2 | Security priority classes of R18 | D33 |
| [V], [I], [E] | Research evidence marks: verified, inference, estimate | R00 |

**Style.** British spelling in prose (licence, localisation, organisation, behaviour); US spelling only in identifiers, file names and quoted sources (`LOCALIZATION.md`, `l10n`). One document status label across the set ("Draft 0.1") until the owner ratifies the register. A decision's status (FIXED, RATIFIED, BINDING, PROPOSED, DEFERRED, SUPERSEDED) lives in section 2.1; other documents write "Proposed (Dn)" only for the record a sentence relies on, and cite "OR-n" for a ruled one. SpaceCorps is the "publisher" throughout; "steward" is reserved for a foundation that might replace it (OR-10).

## 5. Module tiers and release waves

### 5.1 Tiers

T0 kernel: platform, present in every business. T1 must-have: the product is not a business OS without it. T2 second wave: large segments needing vertical depth or compliance. T3 long tail: niche, or best delivered through the extension API. Tier is R01's meaning; wave is when it ships. Any module not listed defaults to T3. The W1 rows are the D86 cut (OR-13, OR-15: the loop and its in and out calls are RATIFIED, the slip order is PROPOSED, section 5.3); modules displaced from W1 appear in the wave they land in, in the order of the slip list of 5.3. Everything from W2 on is an ordered backlog, not a promise: no app is built without a design partner, and nothing in it is sold or withheld (section 6).

| Tier | Wave | Modules | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| T0 | W0 | Kernel crates `obop-types`, `-registry`, `-orm`, `-security`, `-events`, `-jobs`, `-i18n`, `-storage`, `-http`, `-bus-notifier`, `-testkit`; module `base` (the Contacts app: party, principal, company, currency, sequences, external ids, audit, attachments, properties, import/export) | Nothing ships before this |
| T0 | W1 | `product`, `mail` (reduced: chatter, activities, followers, outbound email and templates), `pricing` (list price and line discount through `PricingPort`), `obop-report`; `customize` custom fields (typed command and admin screen); the registry-generated command catalogue with CLI, REST, OpenAPI, MCP and webhooks (D80, D31); the OAuth 2.1 provider, API keys and scoped agent tokens; form, list and kanban views, search, the report viewer and the reconciliation view | R23 foundations and engines, less `account` and `tax`. `customize` is the no-code customisation app (Proposed T0; R01 had it as T2, and R20 keeps declarative custom fields in the first delivery but visual editors out, so only fields are W1). The thin UI is a client of the commands (D80) |
| T0 | W2 | `portal`, `automation` rules and the approvals engine (an engine, not an app), `pricing` rules and price lists, `mail` inbound gateway, aliases and OAuth mailbox connectors (Gmail API, Microsoft Graph, D39), pivot, graph and calendar views, package registry stage 1 (git-backed index with CLI search, install and copy, D81), the web UI parity work that gates 1.0 (D80) | Displaced from W1 in reverse slip order (5.3), plus the parity and registry work. The approvals engine carries the D18 approval policy engine |
| T0 | W3 | `analytics` semantic layer, `analytic`, `resource` (working calendars, leaves, public holidays), `obop-wasm-host`, `obop-ext-sdk`, `obop-compat`, the X4 app model (consent UX, connected-apps inventory), F2 host and UI SDK, theme and branding packs, `customize` custom models and layout editor, `obop-edge`, gantt, map and cohort views, `customize` form builder and print-template designer; package registry service (stage 2, on demand and only after an owner decision that amends D83, W3 or W4) | Moved from W1 and W2 by the OR-13 cut (R24's extension cut and the W2 T0 list); project, timesheet, helpdesk, planning and time off need `resource`; `obop-edge` comes with POS; gantt follows `project` |
| T1 | W1 | `account` (GL, AR/AP, period locks, hash chain, reconciliation, manual payment registration), `tax`, `invoicing`, `payment` (manual only), `bank` (camt.053 and MT940 statement import), `edi` (EN 16931 UBL/CII, Factur-X, XRechnung; issue and receive by email or file), `l10n` framework and `l10n_de` (chart, taxes, UStVA, DATEV EXTF export, GoBD), `crm`, `sales`, `stock` (one warehouse), `sale_stock`, `stock_account` | The ratified smallest honest loop (OR-13, OR-15): order to cash with CRM, bill to pay inside accounting, basic stock with valuation, and the German pack |
| T1 | W2 | `purchase`, `purchase_stock`; Peppol transport adapter (a certified access point of the user's choice, bring-your-own credentials); `payment` SEPA files and Stripe plus one; QuickBooks importer; multi-company (selector, per-company books); `l10n_fr` (Proposed, D4) | Displaced from W1 or kept from the old W2. If W2 overruns, items slip to W3 only in the order of 5.3, which is the one slip order for W2: the PL start first, the FR pack's stable release (`l10n_fr`) second, multi-company third |
| T1 | W3 | The services variant first: `project`, `timesheet`, `sale_project`, `sale_timesheet`, `hr` employee core (employee, version, department, job, calendar; Proposed), `calendar`, `discuss` channels; then stock lots, serials and routes and multi-warehouse, aggregator bank feeds, Xero and ERPNext importers, `hr` (beyond the core, time off), `expenses`, `documents`, `delivery` (`CarrierProvider` trait), `l10n_pl`, fixed assets, deferrals, budgets, to-do | `timesheet` needs an employee record and `resource` calendars, so the employee core ships with it. Moved from W2 by the cut; each item needs a design partner |
| T2 | W3 | `mrp` (with Shop Floor, quality, maintenance), `pos` (retail, DE TSE via a signer the user chooses), `website` (with its block page builder), `ecommerce` (with `loyalty`), `helpdesk`, `planning` (with field work), `subscriptions`, `payroll` engine, `recruitment`, `attendances`, `sign`, `knowledge`, `appointments`, email and SMS marketing, `livechat`, dashboards, `ai` agents, AT and CH packs, `intercompany` | Demand order; `mrp` and `pos` first (after 1.0, so D13's POS capture ships here); each needs a design partner. The CH pack is unresearched, so its wave is open (LOCALIZATION 7.3, question 19). MCP, dry-run and scoped agent tokens are W1 kernel work, not the `ai` module |

| T3 | W4+ or community | Rental, PLM, IoT and VoIP, social and automation marketing, eLearning, forum and blog, events, surveys, fleet, lunch, frontdesk, referrals, appraisals, ESG, equity, marketing card, Amazon/Shopee/Lazada/TikTok connectors, Gelato, repairs, industry bundles, an approvals app | Extension API, the plugin registry (D81) and community |

<a id="52-waves-and-calendar-proposed-d26"></a>

### 5.2 Waves and calendar (D26, D86)

Keep the dates, shrink the scope (OR-13). M0 is 2026-10-01. Only the German dates have a hard external driver (2028-01-01 all-issuer mandate); if M0 slips or capacity falls short, W1 scope compresses along the slip order of 5.3 and 2027-09-30 does not move. The German pack target 2027-09-30 is RATIFIED, and the wave windows and the 1.0 target 2028-04-18 are RATIFIED as kept, on the reading of "keep the dates" in D26; the 0.x release dates, the FR and PL pack dates and the series slots after 1.0 (D85, Q28) are Proposed placements (D26). Fixed external anchors: CEN code lists 2026-11-16, 2027-05-17 and 2027-11-15; Product Liability Directive 2026-12-09; Data Act switching-charge ban 2027-01-12 (a duty of hosts; the sandbox holds no customer data, D83); Microsoft EWS off 2027-04-01; Veri*Factu others 2027-07-01; France SMEs 2027-09-01; CRA main obligations 2027-12-11 (whether they reach a free project is Q5); German all issuers 2028-01-01.

Exit criteria are correctness and adoption criteria that a very small team controls: golden cases green, oracle validation (KoSIT, CEN artefacts), ledger property tests green, chain tests green, the CLI/API/MCP parity test green, and design partners actually using it. None depends on paying customers, ARR, headcount or stars. They are Proposed detail under OR-13.

| Wave | Name | Window | What ships | Exit criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W0 | Foundations | 2026-10-01 to 2026-12-31 | CI baseline with the DCO check; T0 kernel; three spikes read out by 2026-11-27; migration ledger starts; 0.1 preview 2026-12-01 (no stability promise); the command catalogue skeleton and CLI grammar (Q22); security policy and vulnerability-handling runbook v0 | G0 overtaken (the repository is public since 2026-10-26, D26); D6, D7, D9 frozen; pins per D23; 3 to 5 design partners agreed to use it; counsel engaged on Q2, Q5, Q16 and Q26; DATEV and SKR confirmation requested; a throughput review of K1 and K2 against plan (D84) |
| W1 | Loop (D86) | 2027-01-01 to 2027-09-30 | 0.2 (02-02); DE pack alpha 03-31; 0.3 public alpha 04-06 (N-1 guarantee, `obop upgrade --dry-run`, CSV importer); 0.4 05-04; DE beta 06-30; 0.5 public beta 07-06 (no commercial offer; the free hosted sandbox opens with it, D83, OR-20); 0.6 09-07 (Odoo importer); X1 and X2; the generated CLI, REST, OpenAPI and MCP for every shipped module | DE pack stable 2027-09-30: oracles green (CEN artefacts, KoSIT), golden cases green, recorded accountant sign-off; ledger proptests green; no known trial-balance bug; N-1 and N-2 chain tests green; CLI/API/MCP parity test green for every shipped module; the vulnerability-handling runbook drilled by 2027-06-01; at least 3 design partners issue real invoices with it; SpaceCorps' own invoicing runs on it (jurisdiction: Q20). If the oracles, golden cases or sign-off are not there, the pack stays beta: correctness is not traded for the date |
| W2 | Release | 2027-10-01 to 2028-06-30 | 0.7 11-02; CRA technical file 11-30 and sign-off by 2027-12-10 (only if counsel finds SpaceCorps a manufacturer, Q5); cut `release/1.0` 2028-03-07, RC 03-21 and 04-04; **1.0 2028-04-18**, the first stable release with the D85 support window; 1.0.1 05-02; the items displaced from W1, returning in reverse slip order (5.3); package registry stage 1 (D81); the approvals engine; the web UI parity work; multi-company; FR pack stable 03-31 and PL start 2027-10 (both Proposed and first to slip) | 1.0 signed with SBOM and attestation; CLI/API/MCP/UI parity test green (OR-4); the upgrade dry-run and the N-1 chain green; the D85 security-fix process drilled (an advisory and a backport); the DE pack still passes its oracles; the design partners of W1 still use it after the 1.0 upgrade; CRA sign-off if applicable |
| W3 | Breadth | 2028-07-01 to 2029-06-30 | The services variant first, then in demand order and each with a design partner: the extension platform (X3, SDK, compat, F2, the X4 app model), stock extensions, bank feeds, Xero and ERPNext importers, `customize` models and layout editor, T1 `hr`, `expenses`, `documents`, `delivery`, assets; `l10n_pl` (PL pack stable 2028-08-31, Proposed, slips to W4 if W2 overran); the April 2029 series on 2029-04-17 (numbering a placeholder; cadence Proposed, Q28; an October 2028 slot on 2028-10-17 only if Q28 keeps two series a year); T2 apps (`mrp`, `pos` first); F3; custom domains; payroll engine; registry stage 2 on demand and only after an owner decision that amends D83 | At least two T2 apps stable (golden cases, proptests and a design partner each); parity test still green; every shipped pack passes its oracles; advisories handled inside the D85 window |
| W4 | Ecosystem | 2029-07-01 onward | The April 2030 series on 2030-04-16 (numbering a placeholder; cadence Proposed, Q28; an October 2029 slot on 2029-10-16 only if Q28 keeps two series a year); verticals; public-sector track once the EU strategy is law | Governed by the release cadence, not an exit date |

Alignment rules. Scope moves, dates do not (D86). Release labels: the code's core version is already 0.2.0 (D55) and the tags v0.2.0-r1 onward mark the build, so the 0.x labels of this table are product milestones with Proposed labels, and the code's `0.2.0` and `v0.2.0-rN` tags are the running build number until the first milestone is announced (OR-32; the dates stay Proposed under Q8). The 0.5 public beta carries no commercial offer; no plan, price or SLA exists (D2). The free hosted sandbox opens with it (D83, OR-20) and carries no service level; it is an operating duty outside the W1 cut, and its opening is not protected by the slip order of 5.3 (Q35). The vulnerability-handling runbook of D17 is drilled by 2027-06-01, before the public beta. 1.0 falls after the CRA main obligations date (2027-12-11); whether they reach a free project is Q5. Outbound email in W1 uses the operator's SMTP relay or a provider API; the OAuth-only mailbox connectors of D39 (Gmail API, Microsoft Graph) land with the inbound gateway in W2, so the EWS shutdown of 2027-04-01 is not a W1 dependency.

### 5.3 W1 cut: necessary-inside calls and slip order (D86)

The W1 cut is RATIFIED (OR-13, OR-15): kernel, invoicing, accounting with tax, German e-invoicing (XRechnung and Factur-X by email or file) and `l10n_de`, CRM and sales, one-warehouse stock, CLI, REST and MCP, and a thin UI. Out of W1 are purchase orders, the customer portal, Peppol transport, SEPA and Stripe payments, inbound mail and pricing rules; project and timesheet (the services variant) go to W3; the owner pulled nothing back into W1. This subsection holds the in and out calls, which OR-15 confirmed as they stand, and the Proposed detail: the order in which everything else slips, its checkpoints and the landing wave of each displaced item (other than the services variant, W3). Row 16 stays conditional on the D11 limits spike (Q9), and row 21 (locales) stays a Proposed amendment of D34's W1 reading (D86). A call is "in" only if the loop cannot be delivered honestly without it, for one of five reasons: (a) the German pack needs it legally; (b) the ledger's acceptance test needs it (sale, delivery, invoice, payment and books reconcile without manual repair); (c) OR-4 needs it (every shipped capability has CLI, API and MCP, and humans need a minimal UI); (d) the dependency law of D27 needs it; (e) the switcher path of D22 needs it.

**Necessary-inside calls (RATIFIED, OR-15; conditions on rows 16 and 21).**

| # | Call | W1 | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `product` | In | Invoice, quote and stock lines all point at it (d) |
| 2 | `obop-report` (Typst) | In | Invoice, quote and delivery PDFs; Factur-X embeds the XML in a PDF/A-3 (a) |
| 3 | `mail` reduced to chatter, activities, followers, outbound email and templates | In, reduced | Invoices and quotes must be sent and CRM needs activities (b); the inbound gateway and OAuth mailbox connectors leave |
| 4 | Manual payment registration and reconciliation in `account` | In | Matching a payment to an invoice is the ledger's acceptance test (b); SEPA files and Stripe leave |
| 5 | Bank-statement import (camt.053, MT940) in `bank` | In | Accountants reconcile against statements, and file import needs no partner or network (b) |
| 6 | Vendor bills and receipt of EN 16931 invoices (AP) | In | Receiving e-invoices has been mandatory in Germany since 2025-01-01 (a) |
| 7 | `purchase` orders and `purchase_stock` | Out, W2 | Vendor bills suffice for the loop; purchase orders are the first item back and the hinge of the light-trade persona (D4) |
| 8 | `portal` | Out, W2 | Documents reach customers as email with PDF and XML; a portal needs public exposure and payment flows |
| 9 | `customize` custom fields (typed command and admin screen) | In | A design partner cannot adopt an ERP without a custom field, and it proves registry, API, CLI and MCP end to end (c) |
| 10 | The command catalogue with CLI, REST, OpenAPI, MCP and webhooks | In | OR-4; generated from the registry, so cost grows with the catalogue, not with each feature (c) |
| 11 | OAuth 2.1 provider, API keys, scoped agent tokens, dry-run with `plan_id` | In | Agents and the CLI authenticate with them (D18) (c) |
| 12 | Web UI: shell, list, form, kanban, search, report viewer, reconciliation view | In, thin | Accountants and design partners are humans (c); pivot, graph and calendar views leave; full parity comes by 1.0 |
| 13 | CSV importer (0.3) and Odoo importer (0.6: partners, products, taxes, chart, open invoices and bills, opening balances) | In | Switching is the acquisition lever (D22) (e); QuickBooks goes to W2, Xero and ERPNext to W3 |
| 14 | `pricing`: list price and line discount | In, reduced | `sales` reads `PricingPort` (d); price lists and rules leave |
| 15 | DATEV EXTF export, UStVA computation, GoBD export | In | German pack obligations (a); transmission to ELSTER is the user's step or a later adapter, never core |
| 16 | X1 packs; X2 CEL only if the D11 limits spike passes | In | `l10n_de` ships as a pack (a); without the spike result, declarative actions only (Q9) |
| 17 | `automation` rules and the approvals engine | Out, W2 | Webhooks stay with the API; agents use scoped tokens, drafts and dry-run (D18), not an approval engine |
| 18 | `analytic`, `analytics` | Out, W3 | Not needed for the loop; `account` must not depend on `analytic` (to be verified against the R23 graph) |
| 19 | Peppol transport adapter | Out, W2 | E-invoices travel by email or file and Peppol is optional for German B2B (the accountant confirms at sign-off); it returns before 2028-01-01 if capacity allows |
| 20 | X3 host, X4 app model, F0 patches, F2, theme packs, generated SDKs | Out, W3 | R24 s11's extension cut (26 to 34 person-months) does not fit a team of one plus agents (D84) |
| 21 | Locales | en, de | The other target locales of D34 arrive as reviewed community translations |
| 22 | Generated reference docs, `obop upgrade --dry-run`, backup and restore commands, N-1 chain test | In | The 0.3 public alpha needs docs, and D85's promise needs the upgrade path |
| 23 | D30 database integrity and D33 P0 and P1 security items | In, never cut | They cannot be retrofitted |

**Slip order (PROPOSED).** Item 1 is the first to leave when time is short and the last to return. The displaced items land in the wave shown. Items 14 and 15 are in-loop calls (rows 13 and 9 above) that go only once items 1 to 13 are gone.

| Order | Item | Lands | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Extension surfaces beyond X1 and X2: X3, X4 app model, F0 patches, F2, theme and branding packs, SDKs beyond the OpenAPI document | W3 | Largest block, no loop dependency |
| 2 | `analytics` semantic layer | W3 | Needs the catalogue and real data first |
| 3 | `analytic` | W3 | Returns with `project` and `timesheet` |
| 4 | Xero and ERPNext importers | W3 | Smallest switcher audiences |
| 5 | Pivot, graph and calendar views | W2 | The loop runs on list, form and kanban |
| 6 | `automation` rules | W2 | Webhooks stay with the API |
| 7 | QuickBooks importer | W2 | Second switcher path |
| 8 | `portal` | W2 | Email with PDF and XML covers W1 |
| 9 | `payment` providers beyond manual (SEPA files, Stripe plus one) | W2 | Manual registration covers the ledger test |
| 10 | `pricing` rules and price lists | W2 | List price covers the loop |
| 11 | `mail` inbound gateway and OAuth mailbox connectors | W2 | Outbound email covers W1 |
| 12 | Peppol transport adapter | W2 | Optional in Germany; returns before 2028-01-01 if possible |
| 13 | `purchase` orders and `purchase_stock` | W2 | Vendor bills stay; first back |
| 14 | Odoo importer (CSV stays) | W2 | An in-loop call, slipped only if 1 to 13 are gone |
| 15 | `customize` admin screen (the typed command stays) | W2 | An in-loop call, slipped last |

Protected, never slipped inside W1: the kernel, `base` and `product`; D30 integrity and the D33 P0 and P1 items; `invoicing`, `account`, `tax`, manual payments and bank-statement import; `edi` and `l10n_de` with their oracles; `crm` and `sales`; one-warehouse `stock` with `sale_stock` and `stock_account`; the command catalogue with CLI, API, MCP and the parity test; the thin UI; the upgrade dry-run and the N-1 chain test; the vulnerability-handling runbook. Items return in the reverse order: the last to slip is the first back, and W2 takes them in that order as capacity allows. Checkpoints (Proposed): at 0.3 (2027-04-06) and 0.4 (2027-05-04) the owner decides how many items to cut, counting from 1. If the protected core itself is behind at 0.4, 2027-09-30 still stands and the DE pack stays beta until its oracles, golden cases and sign-off are in; correctness is not traded for the date.

**If W2 overruns (PROPOSED; replaces ROADMAP 6.2).** This is the only W2 slip order; other documents cite it and do not restate or reorder it. Slip in this order: (1) PL start; (2) FR pack stable 2028-03-31; (3) multi-company; (4) package registry stage 1; (5) the approvals engine; (6) the returned W1 items, lowest number first. Protected in W2: the 1.0 parity gate, signed artefacts and chain tests, the D85 security-fix process and DE pack maintenance. 1.0 on 2028-04-18 is a kept date (OR-13).

<a id="6-openpaid-boundary"></a>

## 6. Open boundary

**Everything is open and free under Apache-2.0** (OR-1, OR-2). There is no paid side, so the list below describes; it is not a closed list that could ratchet.

- All code: the kernel and every first-party app (accounting, inventory, manufacturing, CRM, HR, payroll engine, POS, helpdesk, documents and the rest, as they are built), the CLI, REST, webhooks, MCP, OpenAPI, importers and full-fidelity export, the package registry client, index and (when built) service, the fleet and release tooling for operators, the SDKs, templates and documentation.
- Authentication (password, TOTP, passkeys, OIDC, LDAP bind), RBAC, record rules and field permissions; the hash-chained audit log with UI and export (13-month default); envelope encryption with local or OpenBao keys; the backup and PITR recipe; a single-business install; multi-company, custom fields and models, Wasm extensions and sideloading.
- BYOK AI, the MCP server, the usage ledger and budget caps.
- Security fixes, SBOMs and signed releases.
- Law-defined pack content: charts, taxes, fiscal positions, VAT-return and audit-file exports, e-invoice syntaxes, validators.
- Business branding, email and PDF templates, portal and website themes and a portal custom domain. Anyone may rebrand a fork, subject only to the trademark policy (Q26).
- Every capability the earlier drafts put on a paid list is an ordinary backlog item: native multi-IdP SAML, full SCIM, WORM or object-lock audit publication, SIEM streaming, legal hold and multi-year audit tiering, auditor evidence packs, cloud-KMS and HSM connectors, customer-managed keys, fleet disaster recovery and cross-region failover, a supported air-gap bundle. Nothing is withheld, and nothing is promised; each is built when someone builds it (section 2.3).

**What is not promised.** Apache-2.0 provides the software as is, without warranty. SpaceCorps does not promise: hosting or any operated service (D83; the free sandbox is a try-out with no promise of availability, and the hosting service planned by OR-22 is not offered yet), support beyond the D85 promise, a service level or response time, a legal-compliance warranty (D38), a legal-change service for country packs, certification, a payroll service, hosted Peppol, KSeF or Veri*Factu gateways, delivery dates beyond section 5, or that any backlog item above is built.

**Services.** The one service planned is the hosting service of OR-22 (D2, D83). It and any other future service (support, certification) are separate from the software and must never restrict it: no feature gating, no withheld or delayed fixes or advisories (CRA Annex I Part II(8); equal-time advisories, D38), no usage beacon, no contract lever on free use. Introducing another needs a published amendment to this section and to D2 (PROPOSED); the amendment sets its own notice period.

**Guard rails that stay.** Security fixes, data export, the API and CLI, multi-company, ledger integrity and the audit log are never gated, because nothing is. There are no Odoo-style contract levers (7% uplift, 25% old-version fee, 300% breach fee, per-100-line fees, usage beacon).

**Replaced.** The paid list, the ratchet, the `ee` conversion rule, the operated-service lines and the licence-grant CLA pledge of the earlier draft are deleted (OR-1, OR-2). D2 records the ruling and section 7 lists the superseded statements.

## 7. Carry-forward corrections

Do not repeat these superseded statements. The first block is technical and dated; the last block lists what the owner rulings of 2026-10-01 (OR-1 to OR-21) superseded, so that writers of companion documents and research stop repeating it.

- **NOTIFY.** R02 SHOULD 14, R06 SHOULD 11, R08, R15 MUST 1, R18, R26 T7 ("post-commit notify") plan LISTEN/NOTIFY as the bus or queue wake-up: use D7.
- **Wasmtime.** R21's "no LTS policy stated": v36 and v48 are LTS lines (24-month support).
- **Rhai.** R05, R15 s11, R06 COULD 23 propose Rhai: CEL only (D11).
- **Odoo offline.** "Read-only cache with 2 GB cap" (R02, R12, R19, R22 first drafts): Odoo 20 queues writes.
- **Field Service.** R01 lists a separate Enterprise app: Odoo 20 merged it into Planning.
- **Prices.** 19.90 and 35.90 are 12-month introductory prices, not plain list prices.
- **Database.** R08 "database per tenant with RLS backstop", R18 "silo and pool first-class", R26 "190k relations": one database per business, about 190k tables per cell.
- **PostgreSQL.** R02 "16+", R18 "16/17/18", R08 and R22 "18 minimum or baseline": 17 and 18.
- **Authorization.** R08, R18 default-allow `ir.rule` with global AND and group OR: Odoo-20 algebra.
- **Edge.** R12 "SQLite everywhere", R18 "Tauri local mode with SQLite": PostgreSQL server, SQLite only as edge store, embedded PostgreSQL for solo.
- **Frontend.** R07 s3.12 and MUST 6 load third-party ESM into the main realm: F1 is trusted code only.
- **PDF.** R15 Chromium pool, R06 SHOULD 9 chromiumoxide: Typst only.
- **Enterprise tier.** R17 s11.2 and SHOULD 9 gate SSO, SCIM, audit, control plane, air-gap, white-label as written: superseded in full, nothing is gated (section 6, OR-2).
- **CLA and pricing.** R05 "avoid a relicensing CLA" and R03 SHOULD 7 "one per-user price": D1, D2 and D82 (no CLA, no price).
- **Deprecation.** R26 T4 and R27 C4 "12 months" are back in force before 1.0 (OR-12), after the 24-month floor was proposed and then withdrawn: D85.
- **Rust.** R06 MUST 1 "move to 1.99 immediately": D23.
- **Rust channel.** "1.99.0 is not yet on the channel", "its manifest returned 404", "1.98.1 as served stable" (research and documents written before 12:38 UTC on 2026-10-01): the stable channel is 1.99.0 (2026-10-01) and 1.98.1 (2026-09-03) was the previous stable. The pin stayed 1.98.1 until 2026-10-15 only because of the D23 waiting rule; `rust-toolchain.toml` pins 1.99.0 now.
- **pnpm versions.** A bare 11.28.2, 11.28.3 or 12.8.1 as "the" pnpm version (R00, R07, ENGINEERING): each belongs to a dist-tag (`latest-11`, `next-11`, `latest`); name the tag, and pin an exact version with `--target package-manager` (section 1.1; Q15).
- **`vp pm`.** "`vp pm` forwards raw package-manager commands", `vp pm sbom` as an unverified forward, `vp pm audit signatures` (R07, R24, R27, ENGINEERING, security-privacy): `vp pm` is a typed command set; `vp pm sbom` and `vp pm licenses` do not exist in vite-plus 1.0.0; write `vp pm audit -- signatures` (I-2; the "`vp` only" ruling is OR-9: two exceptions, the exit drill and `pnpm sbom`; OR-34 adds the mobile toolchain as a third).
- **Native apps and the exception count.** "No native apps beyond the PWA and Tauri shells" (PRODUCT, ux 8, people 8; the research on Tauri mobile, R07 s3.9, is history) and "exactly two exceptions to `vp` only" (CLAUDE.md, ARCHITECTURE, ENGINEERING, GLOSSARY, ux, ROADMAP, README): partly superseded: OR-34 allows React Native (Expo) apps as obop clients and adds the mobile toolchain as a third named exception; the limits to first-party apps and to cases a PWA cannot serve are Proposed (D87). Tauri mobile is still not pursued (Proposed), and the PWA stays the default client.
- **EWS.** "Microsoft turns EWS off on 2027-04-01" without the start: default-disabling begins on 2026-10-01, tenant by tenant and admin-reversible, and EWS is permanently off from 2027-04-01 (D39). On-premises Exchange Server keeps EWS.
- **Names.** The engine is the "approvals engine" (D39), and the approval policy engine of D18 is its policy evaluator; the carrier provider trait is written `CarrierProvider`; event, permission-group, data-class, state and port spellings follow [CONVENTIONS.md](CONVENTIONS.md).
- **Labels.** R24 T1-T5, R25 T1-T3, R26 T1-T13, R23 "T0 (21) and T1 (19)", R20 "Phase": section 4.
- **Manifests.** R02 `module.toml` and R21 `obop.app.toml`: one `obop.toml`.
- **Country order.** R09 "DE, BE, FR, US, NL first": D4.
- **Sidecars.** R10 Karrio sidecar and R15 OCR sidecar are optional, never default (I-1).
- **Odoo dates.** Write "September 2026 (release commit 2026-09-17)"; 19.0 is "September 2025".

**Superseded by the owner rulings (OR-1 to OR-21).** None of the following may be repeated as current. Where a statement names third-party software (the AGPL Stalwart server, ParadeDB `pg_search`, Weblate's GPL), it stays true as a fact about that software.

- **AGPL and the Application Exception.** An AGPL-3.0-or-later core with an s7 Application Exception, Apache-2.0 only for SDKs, WIT worlds, UI SDK and templates, "X5 native crate: AGPL", "in-process plugins are AGPL derivatives", the Wasm/AGPL boundary as a counsel gate, dual-licence exceptions for AGPL-averse buyers (R17 s11, R24 s8, R26 C2 and C4, BUSINESS 3, README 6, ARCHITECTURE 9 and 16 risk AR8): the whole project is Apache-2.0 and the extension boundary is not a licence question (OR-1, D1). In-process third-party Rust stays unsupported for security reasons only (D11).
- **CLA, OSI pledge, veto, covenant.** A licence-grant CLA, the "OSI licence forever" pledge, a steward veto over leaving OSI licences, an investor covenant before a priced round, a signature bot in gate G0 (R17 s4-s5, R26 C3, BUSINESS 3.4, ENGINEERING 8 and Q-E8): DCO sign-off and no CLA (OR-1, D82).
- **Company plus EU non-profit steward.** The company as CRA manufacturer, cloud operator and `ee` owner, a non-profit holding the trademark and the pledge (R17 s7 and s9, R26 C2, BUSINESS 3.5, GLOSSARY "Steward"): SpaceCorps is publisher, copyright holder and trademark holder, a foundation only if counsel later moves it, and the CRA role is a counsel question (OR-10, D1, Q5).
- **Paid list, `ee`, ratchet.** A published, closed, ratcheting paid list; the `ee` tree converting to open after 24 months; native SAML and full SCIM, WORM audit publication, SIEM, KMS and HSM, fleet DR, the supported air-gap bundle and the multi-business control plane as paid; "operated services over open code" (R26 A2, R25 B7, R17 s11.2, R24 s10 "EE" column, BUSINESS 4, PRODUCT 9 and the "Open or paid" lines of the specs, ARCHITECTURE "`ee`" rows): everything is open and free; those capabilities are unpromised backlog items (OR-2, section 6).
- **Prices and plans.** Free, Team (EUR 49), Growth (R26's "Business", EUR 249) and Scale (from EUR 899); per-organisation pricing banded by full users; free light users and three free accountant seats; price tests, ARPA, plan-mix and willingness-to-pay figures (R26 A3 and B, BUSINESS 5, PRODUCT 9, README): there is no price list, plan or pricing unit (OR-2). Odoo's prices remain only as competitor facts (ruling 21).
- **Paid services.** The Assurance DE service, legal-change SLA, capped warranty, payroll service, hosted AI credits at cost plus 20%, hosted Peppol, KSeF and Veri*Factu gateways, certification exam fees, a sold SLA plan and the support tiers "Cloud standard" and "Priority" (R25 B7, R26 A3, R28 D.2, LOCALIZATION 4, ENGINEERING 9.1 and 11.2, BUSINESS 5.3): none exists (OR-2, D17, D85); the hosting service of OR-22 is the only planned paid service and it is not offered yet.
- **Partner and marketplace revenue.** Partner tiers and fees, 20% recurring commission, partner-hosting licence (EUR 6 per business), certified-partner and accountant programmes, localisation partners on revenue share (L2 "paid partners"), marketplace take rate of 0% to 15%, paid listings, payouts and tax (R24 s9-s11, R26 A3, BUSINESS 7, LOCALIZATION 4): none; the registry is free and handles no payments (OR-3, D81).
- **White-label.** A partner white-label trademark licence and reseller console (R24 s10, R26 A2, BUSINESS 7.2 and CN-14): none (OR-2); forks follow the trademark policy (Q26).
- **Hiring gates and team sizes.** R20's 8-12 person team and headcount bands, R26's 4, 6, 10, 17, 33 FTE ramp, the 17-FTE cap before EUR 1M ARR, the cut to 6 FTE at M24, HG0-HG3, GT5 and GT6, the compositions and capacity sums that assume them (R20 s10, R26 B5-B6, ROADMAP 4, 8 and 9): the team is the owner plus AI agents, with no hiring plan or gate (OR-6, D84).
- **Funding model and break-even.** A EUR 3M seed and EUR 5M Series A, a EUR 10.8M peak, break-even at M64, grants as 6% of burn, NLnet as a plan item, the bridge between seed and ARR gate (R26 B5-B7 and C1, BUSINESS 6, BQ12, ROADMAP 9.3 item 3): self-funded, no model (OR-6, D84).
- **SOC 2 and ISO.** SOC 2 Type I at M18, Type II and ISO 27001 at M30, evidence collection from M12 (R26 O1, ROADMAP W2 and W3): no such plan (OR-11; the owner decides whether the hosting service of OR-22 needs one).
- **5-year LTS.** An LTS on every fourth release with five years of support (years 0-2 full, 2-5 security and legal-critical), "1.0 LTS", "2.0 LTS", the LTS-spacing tripwire, the per-branch toolchain and reproducible double build "for LTS" (R27 C3, D16 as drafted, ENGINEERING 4, ROADMAP 7 and 8.2, PRODUCT, GLOSSARY): a 2-year security-fix window from the first stable release, "1.0" and no LTS label (OR-12, D85). Wasmtime's own LTS lines are unaffected.
- **24-month floor and the patch train.** The 24-month deprecation floor and its release-month counting rule, the monthly patch train, hosted-fleet rings as a release promise (R24 s5.3, R27 C3-C7, D16 as drafted, ENGINEERING 4.2-4.6, ROADMAP 7): 12 months before 1.0, the same window after 1.0 unless the owner rules otherwise (D85, Q28).
- **"SpaceCorps hosts nothing" and "no revenue" as permanent rules.** Read these sentences of the companion documents as statements about today. OR-22 (2026-10-27) plans a paid hosting service on dedicated Sliplane servers: it is not offered or charged, but SpaceCorps would become a host, a data processor for its customers and a seller, with the duties OR-22 lists. Do not cite "SpaceCorps hosts nothing" or "no revenue model" as a rule that rules the hosting service out.
- **Hosted operation.** obop Cloud, the paid cloud or paid beta on 2027-07-06, the Free hosted plan and its quotas, dedicated cells as a plan feature, Data Act switching terms in a hosted contract, ToS, DPA and SLA annex, hosted cells and rings, the control plane with billing, own Peppol access point, managed brokers, relays, SMS and routing (R18 s4, R26 B1 and B5, R28 E, BUSINESS 6.1 and CN-11 to CN-13, ENGINEERING 11, LOCALIZATION 4, ARCHITECTURE 3 and 13): SpaceCorps hosts none of that: apart from the free sandbox at the public beta (OR-11, OR-20, D83), the only hosting is the dedicated-server service planned by OR-22, which has no plans, quotas, SLA annex or rings.
- **Milestone words.** "Paid beta", "paid cloud", "SLA plan", "Assurance service", "1.0 LTS", "DE GA": retired (D26, section 4).
- **CRA duties from the first commercial offer.** There is no commercial offer yet (the hosting service of OR-22, once offered, would be one); the CRA role of SpaceCorps is a counsel question (OR-10, Q5), and the CRA-dated milestones (technical file, sign-off) are conditional on it (D26).
- **UI-equals-API ordering.** Any text that builds screens first and exposes the API afterwards, or that calls the API "the same as the UI" without the catalogue behind both (R19, R21, PRODUCT 7, ARCHITECTURE 8 and 10, the specs' per-app "UI then API" phrasing): command first; CLI, API and MCP generated; UI last and at parity before 1.0; a feature is not done until it exists in CLI, API and MCP (OR-4, D80).
- **The W1 list of R20.** The seven-app loop with Purchase, Payments providers and Portal, six view types, X4 remote apps, Peppol through a partner, QuickBooks at 0.6, "CLI v0" and the services variant "if the loop is on schedule at 0.5" (R20 s9.3, PRODUCT 11.1, ROADMAP 2.2 and 6, BUSINESS 1.2 and 8): D86 and section 5.3.
- **Business risks that assumed revenue or hosting.** BUSINESS BR-01, BR-04, BR-06, BR-08, BR-09, BR-13 (answered only by the trademark policy now), BR-14, BR-15, BR-27, BR-28, BR-29, BR-30 and BR-31, and R26's funding, investor, steward, concentration, hosting-competitor, partner, free-tier, accountant-inertia and ARPA risks (F1 to F4, M5 to M9): retired or recast as the schedule-feasibility and key-person risks of D84.
- **The local-demo-only default and the sandbox as a question.** "No hosted sandbox before 1.0", "evaluation through a local demo (`obop dev up`)", "the sandbox is a question (Q27)", "at most a free sandbox" read as still undecided, "Hosted sandbox (D83) | The owner answers Q27", and any cost, duty or risk line that treats a sandbox as something that exists only "if it is ever opened" (the register before OR-20; BUSINESS, ENGINEERING, GLOSSARY, PRODUCT, README, ROADMAP, ARCHITECTURE, CONVENTIONS, cli, ux, platform, integrations and security-privacy): the owner chose a hosted free sandbox at the public beta, with the duties and limits of D83; `obop dev up` is an optional local demo, not the evaluation path of record (OR-20).
- **Proposed status of Q23, Q27, Q29, Q30 and Q31.** "Listing requires an OSI-approved licence (Proposed, Q23)", "what else `verified` requires is Q23", "the human owner reviews and signs off every agent-authored commit (Proposed, Q29)", "the scope of UI parity and the exemption classes are Q30", "recorded exemption means a proposed one", the browser-only act as an exemption class under consideration, and "apply to NLnet Restack by 2026-11-03 (Q31)": the admission rule and the meaning of verified are RATIFIED (OR-17), the sign-off rule is RATIFIED (OR-18), UI parity is literal with an owner-approved exemption list (OR-16), and Q31 is closed with no application (OR-21). Still Proposed: the other admission details of D81 (Q24, Q25) and each entry of the exemption list until the owner approves it. The browser-only act is not an exemption class.
- **RATIFIED with the closure of Q10.** I-1, the D24 renames and the X and L prefixes were recorded as ratified with the closure of Q10, a basis the owner never gave for them (Q10 was closed by OR-8 and OR-9): they are RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-01 (OR-14). Write "RATIFIED (OR-14)". Q10 is closed by OR-8, OR-9 and OR-14, and ENGINEERING Q-E11 is still not covered by it. Rust 1.99.x adoption is a mechanical consequence of D23, not a ruling.
- **W1 "shape RATIFIED, calls PROPOSED".** The in and out calls of 5.3 are RATIFIED (OR-15); only the slip order, its checkpoints and the landing waves of displaced items (except the services variant, W3) remain Proposed. Q8 no longer asks for the W1 calls.
- **Q32 by 2027-03-31.** Q32 is decided at W2 (2027-10-01 to 2028-06-30), with the default of no publication until then (OR-19).
- **Counts and answer dates of closed questions.** "The thirteen rulings", "OR-1 to OR-13", "Q1 to Q33" and "Q22 to Q33", "25 of 32 owner questions open", "seven closed questions (Q1, Q3, Q4, Q7, Q10, Q13, Q19)", and the answer dates of Q23 (2027-09-30), Q27 (2027-06-01), Q29 (2026-12-15), Q30 (2027-03-31), Q31 (2026-10-30) and Q32 (2027-03-31): there are now 34 rulings (OR-1 to OR-34) and 56 questions (Q1 to Q56); 16 are closed (Q1, Q3, Q4, Q7, Q10, Q13, Q15, Q17, Q19, Q23, Q27, Q29, Q30, Q31, Q33, Q42) and 40 are open, reduced or deferred. The new answer dates are Q34 2027-05-04, Q35 2027-03-31, Q36 2027-05-04 and Q32 at W2; Q37 to Q56 carry the event before which each is needed, not a calendar date.

## 8. Open questions for the owner

Q1-Q14 are ranked by irreversibility; Q15-Q20 were added after that ranking; Q21 was added on 2026-10-17 with the build (D40 to D43); Q22-Q33 were added on 2026-10-01 after the first batch of owner rulings (drafted as Q21-Q32 and renumbered, OR-25) and Q34-Q36 after the second (OR-20); Q37-Q42 came with the native-apps ruling of 2026-10-27 (OR-34, D87) and Q43 with the Expo spike of the same day (docs/spec/mobile.md 8.3); Q44-Q56 came with the records of the second wave of the storage foundations on 2026-10-28 (Q44-Q47 with D75, Q48-Q51 with D76, Q52-Q53 with D77, Q54-Q56 with D78). Dates are the latest useful answer; Q37 to Q56 name the event before which each is needed. **Closed by the rulings of 2026-10-01:** Q1, Q3, Q4, Q7, Q10, Q13, Q19, Q23, Q27, Q29, Q30 and Q31, and the country part of Q8 (12 of 56 questions). **Closed by the answers of 2026-10-27:** Q15 (OR-28), Q17 (OR-29) and Q33 (answered by OR-9, the owner did not object) (15 of 56 in all). **Closed on 2026-10-28:** Q42 (OR-34 g, relayed) (16 of 56 in all). **Deferred by ruling:** Q32 stays open and is decided at W2 (OR-19). **Reduced** to what still needs counsel or the owner for SpaceCorps as publisher of a free Apache-2.0 project: Q2, Q5, Q16 and Q20 (and Q6 to the cell size and key type, Q8 to the ICP, the T0 list and the slip order). Everything else keeps its recommended default and stays PROPOSED; 40 questions are open, reduced or deferred. Decide whether that is accepted under the rule that every dependency is under an Apache-2.0-compatible licence and for phones without Google services, or whether the own-module fallback of 4.1 is built on the platform `LocationManager`, which needs no Play services (a FusedLocationProvider module would need them too). Facts read from the installed `expo-location` 58.0.18 (docs/spec/mobile.md 8.7): its default entry, the one the app imports, records with a `FusedLocationProviderClient` (`LocationTaskConsumer.kt`), so the app as built needs Play services at run time; its `expo-location/next` entry has a platform task consumer on the `LocationManager` (`AndroidLocationProvider.kt`, picked by `FallbackLocationProvider.kt` when it is the first available provider), which was not tried; and `play-services-location` stays declared as `api` in its `build.gradle` in both cases, so the licence part of the question stays either way | PROPOSED | Two-way while nothing is published | No default; the spike does not decide it | Before any store release, and before the own-module fallback is designed (no calendar date) |

A tag outside the managed set (`und en de fr pl fi sv es`), or a server without ICU, takes the exact, capped in-memory path instead | PROPOSED | Two-way | Accept AD-1 to AD-4 as listed in D76 Decision 11; the file `accepted_differences.json` and the test that will pin it are not built yet | Before pushdown is switched on by default (no calendar date) |
| Q49 | Behaviour changes of D76: a `shared_with` list is split on eight ASCII separators and nothing is trimmed (already live; a name padded with a non-breaking or other Unicode space no longer matches the person, a list separated by tabs now does); NUL is refused in a text filter value on every backend (already live); a candidate set above 20,000 for a computed filter, an unmapped `lang` or a `public_field` that cannot be compiled is an error that asks for a narrower filter (instead of an unbounded read of memory); a query stopped after 3 s answers HTTP 503 `storage_timeout` with `Retry-After` (not a 400-class error, though the cause can be the user's own filter) | PROPOSED | One-way once clients rely on them (the tokenizer and the NUL refusal are already live) | Accept all four | Before pushdown is switched on by default (no calendar date) |
| Q50 | The default of `OBOP_QUERY_PUSHDOWN` (D76): `on` once the 10,000-query differential run passes on a PostgreSQL release, or `shadow` (which also runs the in-memory path and compares; it reads whole models) for the first release | PROPOSED | Two-way | `on` after the run passes; `shadow` for staging and canaries only | Before the first release that contains the pushdown (no calendar date) |
| Q51 | Retention of the `storage_event` log of D76 (slow and stopped queries; names and shapes, never values) and the PostgreSQL floor: 90 days through the existing purge command (not built), and whether the docs should require PostgreSQL 16 or later (for `EXPLAIN (GENERIC_PLAN)`; D44 already says 17 or 18, and the work has been run on 17 and, in CI, 18) | PROPOSED | Two-way | 90 days; state "PostgreSQL 17 or 18" in docs/DEPLOY.md and no lower floor | When the `storage_event` model is built (no calendar date) |
| Q52 | CLI-only exemption entries for the operator commands of D77 that need credentials the web server must never hold: `storage grants`, `storage split-roles`, `storage guards replace` and `remove`, `modules tamper`, `platform split-roles` (five entries, `guards replace` and `remove` counted as one; they lack the UI, REST and MCP and are listed as a proposed group in docs/spec/cli.md 1.1); and the names of the production switches `OBOP_PRODUCTION` / `--production` and `OBOP_DATABASE_OWNER_URL` / `--owner-db`. Nothing is exempt until the owner approves it (the owner's rule of D73). D75's history maintenance and D78's index build and prune need no exemption | PROPOSED | Two-way | Approve the five exemptions and the four names | Before the first stable release (not before merging) |
| Q53 | Whether a guarded model (D77) may refuse every online write of the kinds that a frozen record cannot take: the sequence backfill, `secrets rotate` of an inventoried sealed field of a guarded model that is not listed in `mutable_fields` (none is guarded today), package `[[migration]]` data steps, GDPR erasure of personal fields (interim: list them in `mutable_fields`; an erasable ticket kind is a follow-up and a legal question), and edits of records on or before the lock date; the re-wrap of sealed history entries by `secrets rotate` (D75) is listed with them under split roles, because the runtime role cannot update history and the re-wrap needs the owner role or a definer function of its own. The owner path is `guards replace` and restore. Not legal advice | PROPOSED | Two-way | Accept: frozen records change only by exit actions, `mutable_fields`, restore and owner replacement | Before the first guarded package is installed on a real instance (no calendar date) |
| Q54 | The amendments of D49 by D78: a call with no `limit` on a model of more than 20,000 records is refused (`limit_required`), an offset above 10,000 is refused (`offset_too_large`), and a call that pages is ordered by id after its own order (an unpaged call keeps insertion order). One setting, `OBOP_QUERY_CANDIDATE_CAP`, controls the first | PROPOSED | One-way once clients rely on the limits | Accept all three | Before the keyset package merges (no calendar date) |
| Q55 | The shape of `list` (D78, D49): the requirement wants `next` on `list` too; D49 rejected changing its shape and the design keeps `list` a bare array (`after`, `total`, `updated_since` and `include_deleted` are `search` arguments and `list` refuses them with `use_search`). Amend D49 so `list` returns an envelope, or keep the bare array | PROPOSED | One-way once clients depend on the shape | Keep the bare array; paging is `search` | Before the keyset package merges (no calendar date) |
| Q56 | The order of ties under a descending sort (D78): a descending sort on a low-cardinality key can cost up to the size of its tie group per page, because the id tie-break stays ascending. Accept that and warn in `obop storage indexes`, or make the id tie-break follow the first key's direction (which changes the in-memory evaluator and the results of existing descending sorts) | PROPOSED | One-way once existing descending results are relied on | Keep the id tie-break ascending and warn | Before the keyset package merges (no calendar date) |

**Companion lists.** Questions that stay in their own documents are two-way details owned there: [ENGINEERING.md](ENGINEERING.md) Q-E4, Q-E5, Q-E7 (is the embedded frontend bundle byte-reproducible) and Q-E10 (and Q-E11, the cargo-fuzz nightly question, which the closure of Q10 by OR-8, OR-9 and OR-14 does not cover), [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) section 9.3 items 6 and 7 and the contributor half of item 8, [BUSINESS.md](BUSINESS.md) BQ7 (drop "fast" from the tagline), [PRODUCT.md](PRODUCT.md) section 14.3 and the open questions at the end of each spec. Folded in or closed by the rulings: Q-E1 (Q17), Q-E2 (Q10, closed by OR-9), Q-E3 (Q7, closed; the residue is Q28), Q-E6 (Q15), Q-E8 (moot: DCO, no CLA), Q-E9 (moot: no paid beta; support runs through community channels), BQ1, BQ3 to BQ6, BQ9 and BQ10 (moot: no plans, partners, steward budget or financial model), BQ2 and BQ11 (Q19, closed), BQ8 (closed by OR-13 and OR-15: the services variant is W3), BQ12 (Q4, closed), BQ13 (Q16), ROADMAP 9.3 items 1 and 3 (moot and Q4), item 2 (Q20), items 4 and 9 (Q28), item 5 (Q16), and PRODUCT 14.3's Free-limit row (moot). Time-only invoicing stays in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) section 16.3 (item 7) and is decided with Q8. [CONVENTIONS.md](CONVENTIONS.md) section 9.1 collects the owner decisions that affect names; each points back to a question of section 8 (for example Q2, Q8, Q11, Q18, Q22, Q25 or Q32), to D27 above, or to an open question of its own spec (its Q10 row is closed, and the Q23 part of its row on what verified requires is answered by OR-17).
